**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (new — the server-side reads of the subscriptions and the plans), `apps/studio/src/app/api/economy/route.ts` (the placeholder becomes real), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (`toPlanRow`, `toPlanRows`, `planPeriodText`), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.ts` (the lane names the account and carries the plans), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.test.ts` (the new signature, the plans, +1 case), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel’s three sections), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §23, and §17’s check with its premise changed).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1126/1126 (61 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **172/172** with the new §23.
**Bounds (§60).** The panel shows the subscriptions this account **holds** and the plans it **offers** — it does NOT show the subscriptions it has **received**: the service has the method (`subscriptionsReceived`) but no door for it, and that is named here rather than half-built. No money moves anywhere in this feature: a subscription is a promise, not a payment, because there is no billing (ADR 0018). The panel reads when it is OPENED and never polls, so a panel left open across an account change would show the previous account’s economy — which is why the proof reloads the page after signing in.
## v310 — the creator’s OTHER side: who backs this account (M7/v310 — ADR 0016 amendment 310)

**The gap.** The service has had `subscriptionsReceived(creatorAccountId, limit)` since v300 — the repository’s finder, the service’s method — and **no door**. So a creator could be backed and could not find out who: the panel showed what this account backs (v309) and what it offers (v309), and not the third half, which is the half a creator most wants to see. The method existing without a door is the same shape as v295’s unfindable reporter: the service could answer a question nobody could ask.
**The door, with its own answer shape.** `GET /subscriptions?creator=` answers with `ReceivedSubscriptionsAnswer(creatorId, subscriptions, totalCents, count, limit)`. The rows are the SAME subscription shape the subscriber’s direction carries — one shape whichever door it came through — and the answer names the CREATOR in its own field, because a list of somebody’s supporters is a list of their own. The total sums the STANDING subscriptions (v308): a promise whose period has run out is not a supporter.
**The panel’s third half.** `Received` sits beside `Subscriptions` and `Your plans`, with its own honest empty state ("nobody backs this account") — and it is a state of its own, because the three halves are three different reads of three different doors and one of them failing must not blank the others.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§24).** The creator opens the panel and sees the subscription they have received, with what the supporter is committed to (¥15.00) → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser (one subscription, and it names the creator).
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (`ReceivedSubscriptionsAnswer`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (the `creator=` direction, and the 400 that names both directions when neither is given), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (`readReceivedSubscriptions`), `apps/studio/src/app/api/economy/route.ts` (the third read), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.ts` (the lane carries them), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the `Received` section), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §24).
Measured: the service’s social package **104/104**, `apps/studio` **1126/1126 (61 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **175/175** with the new §24.
**Bounds (§60).** The received list is opened by NAMING the creator — the same claim-posture as every other door — and a creator’s account id is public, so a caller who knows it can read the list of that creator’s supporters. The rows carry the SUBSCRIBER’s account id (a creator knowing who backs them is the ordinary case), and the panel shows the plan and the amount rather than a list of names — the names are one API call away, and a panel is not the place to put them. The list is capped by the service’s window (100 at the most), so a creator with more supporters than that sees the most recent hundred and the count beside them says which is which. Nothing here moves money: a subscription is a promise, not a payment (ADR 0018).
## v311 — the trail records the REPORT verbs (M7/v311 — ADR 0016 amendment 311)

**The gap.** v289 put the like and the comment into the audit trail — and the trail had not heard of the report since v295. So a complaint — the one social act that asks somebody else to do something — left **no entry at all**: no record that it was filed, and no record that the reporter withdrew it. A complaint nobody can audit is a complaint nobody can answer.
**The service writes both halves down.** `report` when a report is filed, `withdraw-report` when the reporter takes it back. Both are written INSIDE the transaction, like the like and the comment: a rolled-back report leaves no entry claiming a report happened. And only a report that was ACTUALLY filed is written down — a refused duplicate is an attempt, and a trail that recorded attempts would be a trail of things that did not happen. The service’s own cases grade both halves (4 new cases in the report package).
**The client learns the words.** `report` → "reported", `withdraw-report` → "withdrew the report", and — the one the service had been recording since v304 — `acknowledge` → "acknowledged the report". The two halves are NOT folded into one another: "a complaint was filed" and "the reporter no longer stands by it" are different facts, and a trail that merged them would lose one of them.
**The tally gets its own buckets.** The report verbs are counted as `reported` / `withdrew the report` / `acknowledged` rather than falling into `other`, for the same reason the social verbs have their own buckets (v289): a tally that called a complaint "other" would be hiding the very thing a reader of the trail is looking for.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§25).** The trail panel carries the filing, the withdrawal and the acknowledgement → the panel says them in the trail’s own words → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser (`GET /audit` carries all three actions).
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportService.java` (the trail, and the two records), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportServiceTest.java` (the trail’s cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.ts` (the three verbs, the three tally buckets), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+3 cases, and the tally’s shape), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §25).
Measured: the service’s social package **108/108** (the report package 23/23), `apps/studio` **1130/1130 (61 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **180/180** with the new §25.
**Bounds (§60).** **The verbs whose target is NOT a publication are still out of the trail**, and the reason is the same one the report’s identity is kept out of the wire (v307): the trail’s target column means "the publication this happened to", so a row saying "followed acct-ada" in that column would be a small lie. The follow, the tip (both about an ACCOUNT) and the plan/subscribe (about a PLAN) would need the trail to say what KIND of thing the target is — a column the audit’s table does not have — and adding one is a schema change, not a line of service code. So the trail is named here as a bound and left for an increment that can carry a migration. The trail still names ACCOUNT IDS and not handles (v270’s finding: the service has no account table), so a reader of a report’s entry sees who filed it and not what they are called.
## v312 — the reports TALLYED by reason (M7/v312 — ADR 0016 amendment 312)

**The gap.** The reasons are a CLOSED SET of four (v295), and the desk (v304) showed the rows — but nothing counted them. "Three reports" says less than "two spam and one harassment": a creator cannot tell a nuisance from a pattern, and the reasons are a closed set precisely so that they CAN be counted. The desk’s rows are also a WINDOW (the most recent hundred), so a count taken from them would be a count of the window.
**The tally counts EVERY report, from a GROUPED query.** `ReportRepository.countByTargetsGroupedByReasonAndStatus` asks the database for `(reason, status, count)` over every report against the account’s work — one row per pair that occurs. The service adds them up into two MARGINAL totals (by reason, by status) and the total and the open count. It is a door of its own (`GET /moderation/tally?owner=`) rather than a field on the desk, because the desk is a window and the tally is not.
**The panel shows it above the rows.** The tally rides along with the desk in ONE read (the Studio’s own door asks for both), so a creator sees "2 report(s) · 1 spam · 1 harassment" and the rows under it. A reason that does not occur is NOT said: a tally padded with zeroes is noise, and a reader learns to skip it.
**The service’s own cases grade the three things that matter (4 new).** The tally counts by reason AND by status; it counts EVERY report and not the desk’s window (a case that answers 30 and asserts 30 — the number that is wrong is the one a creator acts on); an account with no publications has an empty tally; and a caller that names no account is refused by name.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§26).** The desk shows the tally → the tally counts the reports BY REASON → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser (two reports: one spam, one harassment).
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportRepository.java` (the grouped finder), `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/moderation/ModerationService.java` (`tallyOf`), `.../moderation/ModerationDtos.java` (`TallyAnswer`), `.../moderation/ModerationController.java` (`GET /moderation/tally`), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/moderation/ModerationServiceTest.java` (+4 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.ts` (`TallyFacts`, `tallyText`), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationServer.ts` (`readTally`), `apps/studio/src/app/api/moderation/route.ts` (the tally rides along with the desk), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationLane.ts` (the lane carries it), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the tally line), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §26).
Measured: the moderation package **16/16**, the service’s social package **112/112**, `apps/studio` **1133/1133 (61 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **183/183** with the new §26.
**Bounds (§60).** The two maps are MARGINAL totals: the answer says how many reports there are by reason and how many by status, and NOT the cross ("of the two spam reports, one is still open"). The cross is a bigger answer than a creator needs — the open count beside it is the number they act on — and the raw rows are one query away for anybody who wants it. The tally covers the owner’s most recent 200 publications, the same window the desk covers (v304’s bound), so a creator with more than that sees the tally of their recent work and not of all of it. And a tally that could not be read stays NULL in the client: a tally of zero would say "nobody complained", which is the one thing a creator must not be misled about.
## v313 — a subscription that ENDED says so, and the refusal keeps the service’s own words (M7/v313 — ADR 0016 amendment 315)

**The gap.** v308 computed the subscription’s STANDING, and the service refused a second subscription with "this account already backs this plan" — which is a thing worth saying only while the subscription is standing. A subscription whose period ran out, or which the supporter cancelled, does **not** back the plan any more: the refusal was telling the reader something false about a subscription that had ended months ago, and the reader would go looking for a subscription that is not there.
**The two refusals are told apart, and each says what happened.** A subscription that is still standing → "this account already backs this plan" (true ✓). A subscription that was CANCELLED → "this account cancelled this subscription, and it cannot be made again: one subscription per supporter per plan is the DATABASE’s rule". A subscription whose period RAN OUT → the same refusal with the date it ended on, and with the way out: "a creator can offer a NEW plan, and the subscription would be made against that."
**And the panel says why there is no button to press.** A subscription row that has ended carries a note — "this build cannot renew it: one subscription per supporter per plan is the database’s rule, and there is no billing" — because a panel that said only "Expired" leaves a reader hunting for a renewal button that does not exist. The standing is computed ONCE per row (v308 computed it three times, and three computations of the same thing across a clock tick could disagree with each other).
**THE BUG THE PROOF FOUND: the service’s refusal words were being thrown away, in SEVEN places.** The E2E read "Could not back pro: **the answer was HTTP 400**" — the service’s own sentence had vanished. The cause: the service’s error body is `{error, message}` and carries **no `ok` field**, and five server-side halves (the likes, the follows, the reports, the tips, the plans) only looked for the reason when the body said `ok: false` — so every refusal they forwarded said "the X answered HTTP 400" instead of the rule that was broken. The two lanes with the same shape (the likes, the follows) were fixed the same way. **A refusal that loses its reason is a refusal that has to be run again to learn anything**, and the studio’s server halves had no tests at all — which is why it survived since v284. There is now one (`planServer.test.ts`, 4 cases), and it grades the rule directly: a refusal keeps the service’s own words even though the body carries no `ok` field.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§27).** The supporter ends their own subscription → tries to back the plan again → the Studio’s own words carry the service’s sentence ("this account cancelled this subscription, and it cannot be made again") → and the panel shows the note that says why there is no button to press.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionService.java` (the two refusals), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the `renewNote`, and the standing computed once), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the note under the row), `apps/studio/src/lib/likeServer.ts`, `followServer.ts`, `reportServer.ts`, `tipServer.ts`, `planServer.ts`, `likeLane.ts`, `followLane.ts` (the refusal’s words survive), `apps/studio/src/lib/planServer.test.ts` (new), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §27).
Measured: the service’s social package **101/101** (the subscription package 27/27), the moderation package **16/16**, `apps/studio` **1140/1140 (62 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **187/187** with the new §27.
**Bounds (§60).** **The renewal itself is still not built, and the reason is the schema.** "One subscription per supporter per plan" is the DATABASE’s unique constraint, and changing it means a rule that depends on TIME (only the STANDING rows are unique) — which a unique index cannot express, because an index predicate must be IMMUTABLE and `now()` is not. So a renewal needs either a trigger or a different table, and this build has **no migration tool** (the service’s own configuration says so: `ddl-auto: update`, and "a migration tool is the honest answer once a destructive change exists"). Until that exists, a subscription that has ended is a dead end — and this increment makes the dead end VISIBLE and honest instead of shipping a renewal that overwrote a fact about the past. Nothing here moves money: a subscription is a promise, not a payment.
## v314 — a creator can retire their own plan (M7/v314 — ADR 0016 amendment 316)

**The gap.** v301 built the plans and the subscriptions, and the service could OFFER a plan — but there was no way to take one back. So a creator who changed their mind (or stopped doing the work) had a plan sitting in the catalogue forever, and the only thing a supporter could do about it was to read it. A plan is an OFFER, and an offer with no way to withdraw it is a promise the creator cannot keep.
**The door: `DELETE /plans/{planId}?claimer=` — and only the creator’s own.** The service compares the claim with the plan’s creator and refuses anybody else’s plan with a 403 (a well-formed request that is understood and refused — the house’s own rule ✓). A plan that does not exist is "already gone", and a plan that is already retired is "already retired" — neither is a failure, because the URL names a plan rather than a resource that must exist.
**Retiring takes the OFFER away and leaves the PROMISES alone.** The subscriptions already made against the plan keep standing: a supporter who backed the plan for a month was promised that month, and a creator changing their mind about the offer does not take the promise with it. The service never touches the subscription rows on a retirement — and the service’s own case says so by asserting that the subscriptions are never written (a case that would fail the moment someone made a retirement cascade). And nobody new can back a retired plan: the service already refused a subscription to a plan "that is not offered any more", and v314 makes that reachable.
**The Studio’s panel.** `Your plans` grows a **Retire** button on the plans that are still offered — and the row says which state the plan is in ("offered" / "retired"), because a plan that cannot be offered any more and a plan that was never there are different facts. The panel’s note says whether the call retired the plan or found it already retired — a panel that said "retired" either way would claim a change that did not happen.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§28).** The creator sees the plan they offer → takes the offer away → the panel shows the plan as retired → the SERVICE agrees (read independently of the browser) → and nobody can back it any more: the service refuses by name.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/PlanEntry.java` (`markRetired`), `.../social/NotYourPlan.java` (new — the 403), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (`retire`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (the door and the 403 handler), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+5 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/planServer.ts` (`retirePlan`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.ts` (`retirePlan`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.test.ts` (new — 4 cases), `apps/studio/src/app/api/plans/route.ts` (a DELETE beside the POST), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the button, the row’s state, the note), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §28).
Measured: the service’s social package **106/106** (the subscription package 32/32), `apps/studio` **1144/1144 (63 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **191/191** with the new §28.
**Bounds (§60).** Retiring is not cancelling: the subscriptions keep standing and the supporter is not refunded, charged, or told anything (there is no billing and no mail in this build — ADR 0018). A plan can be retired and it cannot be un-retired — the row’s `active` flag is a one-way fact, and offering the same plan id again is refused by the plan id’s own uniqueness. The creator’s claim is a CLAIM (the same posture as the take-down’s): the service compares the account id it was given with the row’s, and the account id is public — so a caller who knows a creator’s id can retire that creator’s plan, and the build says so rather than pretending otherwise. There is no grace period and no `unretire`: a creator who wants the offer back offers a NEW plan id.
## v315 — the holder can END a subscription (M7/v315 — ADR 0016 amendment 317)

**The gap.** The service has had the ending since v300 (`DELETE /subscriptions/{id}?claimer=`, and it refuses somebody else’s subscription by name) and the panel had **no button** for it. So a supporter who wanted to stop backing a plan had no way to say so from the product — the promise simply stood, and the only way to be rid of it was to end the DEVICE’s account and lose everything else with it.
**Ending is not deleting.** The row STAYS and its standing becomes `cancelled`, because a promise that was ended is a fact that happened — the same rule the taken-down publication and the withdrawn report keep. The panel’s button appears only on the rows that are still STANDING: an expired or an ended subscription has nothing left to end, and a button that would do nothing is a button that lies.
**The button names the SUBSCRIPTION, not the plan.** Two subscriptions of the same plan are two promises, and a panel that could only name the plan could not end one of them — so the row carries the subscription’s own id (the wire already had it ✓) and the button carries that.
**The panel says whether the call MOVED anything.** "ended" and "was already ended" are different facts, and a panel that said "ended" either way would claim a change that did not happen — the same rule the report’s withdrawal and the plan’s retirement follow ✓.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§29).** The creator offers a FRESH plan (§27 ended the only subscription there was and §28 retired the plan it was made against — so the section builds its own) → a supporter backs it → the panel offers the holder the ending → the subscription shows as ended, with the row still there → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser (the row’s status is `cancelled`).
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/planServer.ts` (`cancelSubscription`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.ts` (`cancelSubscription`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/app/api/subscriptions/route.ts` (a DELETE beside the POST), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the row carries the subscription’s own id), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the button, the note), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §29).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1147/1147 (63 files)**, the service’s social package **106/106** (unchanged — this increment is the client’s half of a door the service already had), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **196/196** with the new §29.
**Bounds (§60).** Ending a subscription does not refund, charge, or notify anybody — there is no billing and no mail in this build (ADR 0018), and the service’s `cancel` refuses with a 400 rather than a 403 (a well-formed request that names somebody else’s subscription) — which is the package’s own older shape ✓, and it is named here rather than quietly changed. The panel’s button is on the SUBSCRIPTIONS THIS DEVICE HOLDS — the ones the account paid for with a claim — and the service’s comparison is the same claim-posture as everywhere else: the account id is named, and the id is public. There is no `un-end`: a subscription that was ended stays ended, and backing the plan again is the database’s one-per-plan rule (v313’s bound ✓).
## v311 — the trail records the REPORT verbs (M7/v311 — ADR 0016 amendment 318)

**The gap.** v289 put the like and the comment into the audit trail, and the report — the one social act that asks somebody ELSE to do something — had never been there. So a complaint left **no entry at all**: no record that it was filed, and no record that the reporter withdrew it. A complaint nobody can audit is a complaint nobody can answer, and the trail is where a reader looks for what happened.
**The service writes both halves down.** `report` when a report is filed and `withdraw-report` when the reporter takes it back — INSIDE the transaction, like the like and the comment: a rolled-back report leaves no entry claiming a report happened. And only a report that was actually filed is written down: a refused duplicate is an ATTEMPT, and a trail that recorded attempts would be a trail of things that did not happen. The service’s own cases grade both (2 new in the report package): the filing writes, the withdrawal writes, and a REFUSED filing writes nothing.
**The panel learns the words.** "reported", "withdrew the report", and — the one the service has recorded since v304 — "acknowledged the report". Until now that last one appeared as the raw action, which is honest but reads like a machine; the raw word was the right answer for a verb this build had no word for, and now there IS a word.
**The tally gets its own buckets** (`reported` / `withdrew the report` / `acknowledged`), for the same reason the social verbs have their own (v289): a tally that called a complaint "other" would be hiding the very thing a reader of the trail is looking for. The tally still says only the buckets that are NOT ZERO, so it does not grow a line of zeroes as the verbs arrive.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§25).** The trail panel carries the filing, the withdrawal and the acknowledgement → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser (`GET /audit` carries all three actions) — because the panel could be showing its own copy.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportService.java` (the trail, and the two records), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.ts` (the three words, the three buckets), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+2 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §25).
Measured: the service’s social package **112/112** (the report package 23/23), `apps/studio` **1128/1128 (61 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **200/200** with the new §25.
**Bounds (§60).** **The verbs whose target is NOT a publication are still out of the trail** — the follow and the tip (both about an ACCOUNT) and the plan and the subscription (about a PLAN) — and the reason is the same one the report’s identity is kept out of the wire (v307): the trail’s target column means "the publication this happened to", so a row saying "followed acct-ada" in that column would be a small lie. They would need the trail to say what KIND of thing the target is — a column the audit’s table does not have — which is a schema change, and this build has no migration tool (its own configuration says so). So the trail is named here as a bound and left for an increment that can carry a migration. The trail still names ACCOUNT IDS and not handles (v270’s finding: the service has no account table to resolve them with), so a reader of a report’s entry sees who filed it and not what they are called.
## v316 — YOU FOLLOW: and the one way to take a follow back (M7/v316 — ADR 0016 amendment 319)

**The gap.** The un-follow has existed since v288 — the intent (`/?unfollow={accountId}`), the lane, the Studio’s door and the service’s own door (which refuses somebody else’s edge by name). **Nothing in the product pointed at it.** A capability with no door is a capability nobody can reach, and a reader who cannot undo a follow has a page that only ever grows: every follow is permanent, and the graph’s edge count is a number you can only increase.
**The desk’s third section: YOU FOLLOW.** The accounts THIS device follows, each a link to that account’s profile, and beside each one an **Unfollow** button. The desk is where the device’s own social facts live — what came to you (the reports), what you sent (the complaints you filed) and, now, who you follow — and the follow is the only one of the three a reader can take back. The button appears on the device’s OWN list, because the Studio knows who the device is; the server-rendered profile page does not, and a page that showed an "Unfollow" button on somebody else’s follows would be offering a button that always fails.
**The read, and the rule it keeps.** The Studio’s `/api/follows` grew a GET beside its POST (`?follower=`), and the browser half (`readMyFollowing`) answers with a REASON and never a thrown error. **A failed read is NOT an empty list**: "this device follows nobody" and "the list could not be read" are different facts, and a panel that showed the first when it knew the second would tell a reader their follows had gone — the same rule the desk’s tally keeps (v312) and the same rule the rest of the studio keeps.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§30).** The desk shows who this device follows → the Unfollow button takes it back → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: the edge is GONE (`?follower=` answers with an empty list).
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/app/api/follows/route.ts` (a GET beside the POST), `apps/studio/src/lib/followLane.ts` (`readMyFollowing`), `apps/studio/src/lib/followLane.test.ts` (new — 3 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the `YOU FOLLOW` section, the un-follow, the note’s hook), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §30, and the duplicate §25 a re-run had left behind removed).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1150/1150 (64 files)**, the service’s social package **112/112** (unchanged — this increment is the client’s half of doors the service already had), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **199/199** with the new §30.
**Bounds (§60).** The un-follow is a CLAIM, exactly like every other write here: the studio sends the account id the device holds and the service compares it with the row’s follower — which is why the button is on the device’s OWN list and why the service refuses somebody else’s edge by name (a 403, `not-your-follow`). The PROFILE page still cannot offer the button: it is server-rendered and does not know who the reader is (ADR 0018), so the un-follow lives in the desk rather than on the profile. And the desk is now three sections wide (what came to you, what you sent, who you follow) with the tally above them — a panel that keeps growing is a panel that stops being read, and if a fourth section ever arrives it should be a new panel rather than a fourth block here.
## v317 — the creator can CHANGE the price of a plan (M7/v317 — ADR 0016 amendment 320)

**The gap.** A creator could OFFER a plan (v301) and RETIRE it (v314) — and there was no way to change its price. So a plan’s price was fixed for ever, and a creator whose work got more (or less) valuable had no way to say so: the only honest move was to offer a NEW plan id, and the old one sat in the catalogue beside it for ever. The price is a SNAPSHOT precisely so that a change can be made (v300) — and this is the door that makes it.
**The rule, and the thing it does NOT do.** `PUT /plans/{planId}?claimer=&price=&period=` — and only the creator’s own plan (a 403, `not-your-plan`, by name). **A price change does not touch the subscriptions already made**: each one holds the price as a snapshot, so a supporter who agreed to ¥15 a month still agreed to ¥15 a month, and the new price is what the NEXT subscriber agrees to. The service’s own case says so by asserting that the subscriptions repository is never written — a case that would fail the moment someone made a price change cascade.
**The surface.** The account panel’s plan form grows a second button — **Change the price** — beside **Offer**, on the same three fields (the plan id, the price in yuan, the period). One form, two verbs, and the form says which is which: a creator typing a plan id they already offer and pressing "Change the price" is changing the price, and pressing "Offer" is offering a new plan (which the service refuses if the id is taken). The panel’s note says what happened, including the part that matters: "the subscriptions already made keep the price they were agreed at".
**What the real-browser proof grades (§31).** The creator changes the price of a plan they offer → the panel says the new price and says that the old subscriptions did not move → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: the plan now costs the new price. (The snapshot itself is graded by the service’s own case — the proof’s subscriptions had all been ended by the sections before it, so there was no standing one to watch stay put.)
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/PlanEntry.java` (`reprice`), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (`reprice`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (the `PUT` door), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+5 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/planServer.ts` (`repricePlan`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.ts` (`repricePlan`), `apps/studio/src/lib/planLane.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/app/api/plans/route.ts` (a PUT beside the POST and the DELETE), `apps/studio/src/components/AccountPanel.tsx` (the second button), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §31).
Measured: the service’s social package **117/117** (the subscription package 37/37), `apps/studio` **1152/1152 (64 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **201/201** with the new §31.
**Bounds (§60).** A price change is the creator’s word about what their work costs from now on, and it is still a CLAIM (there is no session in this build — ADR 0018): the service compares the account id it was given with the plan’s creator, which is exactly as strong as the take-down’s owner check, and exactly as weak. The currency does not change (a plan’s currency is set when it is offered), a RETIRED plan may be repriced but stays retired (retiring is one-way), and there is no history of prices: the plan carries the price it has NOW, and the subscriptions carry the prices they were agreed at — which is where a price’s history lives, as a fact per subscription.
## v318 — the desk filters by REASON (M7/v318 — ADR 0016 amendment 321)

**The gap.** The reasons are a closed set of four (v295) and the desk’s tally counts them (v312) — so the panel could already say "two spam and one harassment" — but a creator who wanted to LOOK at the spam ones had to read every row to find them. A count that cannot be turned into a list is a number, not a door: the tally made the reasons legible and then stopped there.
**The filter.** One button per reason, and **only for the reasons that OCCUR**: a button for a reason with no reports is noise, and a reader who has learned to skip the whole row will not look again. The filter asks in the **service’s own word** (the raw reason — `spam`, `harassment`, `illegal`, `other`), not in the display word, the same rule the trail’s filter follows: a filter that matched on the translation would break the moment the translation changed, and the display word is a translation.
**The tally is NOT filtered.** The tally keeps counting the whole answer, so the filter never hides anything without a number saying it is there — v281’s rule, the one the trail’s tally already keeps. Both are on screen at once, and the proof grades exactly that: filtering narrows the list, and the tally still names the reasons that are now off screen.
**A filter that matches nothing says so.** "no reports with this reason" — because an empty list under a full tally reads as "the rows are still loading", and a reader who asked for one reason and saw nothing deserves to be told that the desk HAS reports, just not of that one.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§32).** The desk offers a filter for each reason that occurs → filtering by a reason narrows the LIST → the TALLY still counts the whole answer → and "All" brings every row back.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.ts` (`filterDeskRows` — pure, and it hands back a COPY so a caller cannot narrow the desk in place), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.test.ts` (+4 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the filter row, the narrowed rows, the empty note), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §32).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1156/1156 (64 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — this increment is the client’s half of a view the data already supported), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **207/207** with the new §32.
**Bounds (§60).** **The filter is a VIEW, not a filter in the service**: the whole desk still comes over the wire and the rows are narrowed in the browser, so a desk with a thousand reports would still read a thousand — and the service’s desk door takes no reason parameter. Moving the narrowing to the service is a door that does not exist yet (it would be a query parameter the service would have to validate against its own closed set), and it is named here as the next step rather than done. The buttons wear the service’s own words on purpose: a paraphrased label would need a second mapping table, and the mapping a reader needs is the tally’s. And the filter is **not remembered**: the panel holds it in the component’s state, so a reader who filtered yesterday and comes back finds the whole desk — deliberately, because a filter remembered from last week, applied to a desk that has changed since, answers a question nobody asked.
## v319 — the trail filters by the REPORT verbs (M7/v319 — ADR 0016 amendment 322)

**The gap.** The trail has recorded the report verbs since v311 — the filing, the withdrawal and the owner’s acknowledgement — and the panel has counted them in its own summary since the same increment. But the trail’s FILTER stopped at the comment (v289’s social verbs): the three report actions were visible only under "All". So a reader asking the question the trail exists to answer — "was my work complained about?" — had to read the whole trail to find out, and the one action an owner ACTS on (the acknowledgement) was the hardest to find of the three.
**Three more buttons: Reported / Withdrew / Acknowledged.** Each asks in the trail’s own **action** (`report`, `withdraw-report`, `acknowledge`), the same raw word every other button on that row has used since v281. The row now **wraps**: eight buttons do not fit in one line of a 400px panel, and a filter row that overflows its panel pushes its own summary out of sight — the summary is the part that says what the filter is hiding.
**The label wears the display word; the filter asks in the raw action.** The buttons read "Reported" (the display word is "reported") and the filter takes `report` (the service’s action). Those are two different strings on purpose, and a unit case now pins the difference: **filtering by "reported" matches nothing**. If the two were the same string, a rename of the display word would silently change what the filter means — and a filter that suddenly matched nothing would look like a trail that suddenly went empty.
**The summary still counts the whole trail** (v281’s rule, the one the desk keeps too): the filter narrows the list, the summary keeps the total, and both are on screen at once.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§33).** The trail offers a filter for every action it records, and the report verbs are among them → filtering by an action shows ONLY that action → the summary still counts the whole trail → and "All" brings every row back.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (three buttons, and the row wraps), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+2 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §33).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1158/1158 (64 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — the service has recorded these actions since v311; this increment gives them a door in the panel), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **212/212** with the new §33.
**Bounds (§60).** **The trail’s filter still has no buttons for the two undoings the service records** — `unlike` and `uncomment` — so "who took their like back?" is still an "All" question; the row is already two lines deep and the undoings are the least-asked-for pair, so they are named here and left for the increment that can afford them. The filter is a VIEW over the whole trail: the service’s `/audit` takes no action parameter, so the narrowing happens in the browser over a page the service already sent — the same shape as the desk’s reason filter (v318), and the same next step for both (a parameter the service would have to validate against its own set of actions). And the buttons are stable rather than dynamic: unlike the desk’s reason filter, which offers only the reasons that OCCUR, the trail offers every action it knows, so a reader can press a button before anything of that kind has happened — and the panel says "no entries of that action" rather than pretending the button was always there.
## v320 — the UN-LIKE gets a door in the product (M7/v320 — ADR 0016 amendment 323)

**The gap.** The un-like’s chain was already whole: the intent (`/?unlike={id}` ✓), the lane ✓, the Studio’s own door ✓, the service’s door ✓, and the service’s own rule that somebody else’s like cannot be taken back ✓. **Nothing in the product pointed at it.** The artifact page’s bar offered exactly one link — "Like" — so a reader who had liked a publication could never take the like back: the count was a number only the reader could increase, and the trail’s `unlike` verb (recorded by the service since v289) was a fact that no reader could ever cause. A capability with no door is a capability nobody can reach — the same shape the un-follow had before v316.
**The bar offers the taking-back.** A second link, `/?unlike={id}`, beside the like — the same arrangement (a link to the Studio, where the account lives) and the same wording discipline: the page still **cannot say whether YOU liked it**, so the link is an OFFER and not a claim. The bar’s own rule — "a button that says ‘Liked’ when the liker is unknown would be a claim the page cannot support" — is graded: the bar’s HTML carries no "liked" at all, and a case says so.
**The Studio’s words were the liar, and now they are not.** The service answers the same way whether there was a like to take back or not — "a like that is already gone is already gone" is its own rule — so the old message, "took the like back on X", said something untrue for a reader who had never liked it. The message is now "**the like on X is gone — N like(s) now**", which is true in both cases. The existing proof’s check was updated to grade the new words, and it grades the SHAPE of them, not a phrase that can drift.
**The trail can now ask for it by name.** The filter row gains a **Like taken back** button (`unlike`), because the service has recorded that fact since v289 and the product can now cause it: a filter that could not name a recorded verb would leave it in the "All" bucket with no way to say "just the takings-back".
**What the real-browser proof grades (§34).** The bar offers the taking-back → the device likes the publication again → the SERVICE counts it → the device takes it back → the SERVICE agrees the like is gone → the trail offers a filter for the taking-back → and filtering by it shows ONLY the taking-backs. The section runs a whole cycle of its own, so the counts and the trail are graded on this section’s facts and not on the ones the earlier section happened to leave behind.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/likeBar.ts` (the second link, its style, and the bar’s rule), `apps/studio/src/lib/likeBar.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Studio.tsx` (the honest words), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the trail’s new button), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+1 case), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §34, and the existing un-like check’s words updated).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1161/1161 (64 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — the service already had the door and the rule), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **219/219** with the new §34.
**Bounds (§60).** The bar offers the taking-back to EVERY reader, including one who never liked the publication — the page cannot know, and the service’s answer (and the Studio’s new words) are what tell the truth about it; the alternative — a bar that only offered the taking-back to somebody who had liked it — would need the page to know who is asking, which is ADR 0018’s session, not this build. The service’s idempotency is untouched: taking a like that is already gone is not an error, and no trail entry is written for it, because a thing that did not happen is not a fact to record. And **the other undoing is still shut**: the service records `uncomment` (v289) and the studio has no door, no intent and no link for it — a comment its author cannot take back is the same gap this increment closed for the like, and it is named here as the next one.
## v321 — the UN-COMMENT gets a door in the product (M7/v321 — ADR 0016 amendment 324)

**The gap.** The service has had the take-back of a comment since v289 — `DELETE /comments/{id}`, the author’s own rule, the trail’s `uncomment` verb — and the STUDIO had **no door at all**: no server half, no lane, no route, no intent, no link. So a comment its author could not take back was a comment that could only be lived with, and the trail’s `uncomment` verb was a fact no reader could ever cause. This is the same shape the un-like had one increment ago (v320), and the same shape the un-follow had before v316: a capability with no door is a capability nobody can reach.
**The chain, built end to end.** The server half (`removeCommentOnService`) → the browser half (`removeComment`) → the Studio’s own door (`DELETE /api/comments/{commentId}?claimer=`, a DYNAMIC SEGMENT, because a route that reads a path parameter has to BE a path parameter — v294’s lesson) → the intent (`/?uncomment={id}`) → the Studio’s handling → and the artifact page’s comments section, which now carries a **Take it back** link on every comment that is still standing.
**The link is an OFFER, not a claim.** The page is server-rendered and cannot know who is reading (ADR 0018), so it does not say "your comment" and it does not hide the link from somebody else’s comment: every standing comment carries the link, and the SERVICE is the one that refuses somebody else’s comment by name. A comment that is ALREADY removed carries no link — there is nothing to take back, and offering it would be offering a thing that does not exist.
**The words are the service’s own truth.** The service answers 200 whether a comment was taken back or was already gone, and it says which: `removed` is true when a row was taken back. So the Studio says **two different sentences**: "took the comment back — the thread now says it was removed by its author", and "that comment was already gone". A client that dropped the flag would say "took it back" about a comment nobody took back — the same lie the un-like’s message used to tell (v320).
**What the real-browser proof grades (§35).** The artifact page’s comments section offers the taking-back → the Studio takes the comment back → the SERVICE agrees: the comment comes back marked as removed and the VISIBLE count drops → and the trail carries the `uncomment` → and the service’s own two answers are the two the client words differently.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/commentIntent.ts` (`uncommentIdFromSearch`), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentServer.ts` (`removeCommentOnService`), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentLane.ts` (`removeComment`), `apps/studio/src/app/api/comments/[commentId]/route.ts` (new — the DELETE door), `apps/studio/src/components/Studio.tsx` (the intent, and the two honest sentences), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentBar.ts` (the link, and its style), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentBar.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentLane.test.ts` (new — 3 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §35).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1167/1167 (65 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — the service already had the door and the rule), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **224/224** with the new §35.
**Bounds (§60).** The take-back is a CLAIM, exactly like every other write in this build: the studio sends the account id the device holds and the service compares it with the comment’s author — which is exactly as strong as the like’s owner check, and exactly as weak (there is no session: ADR 0018). The link is offered to every reader, so the common case is a refusal that names itself — and the client shows the service’s words, not a status code. And the **trail’s filter still cannot name the `uncomment`**: the verb is now reachable ✓, and the filter row has no button for it — the same one-line gap the un-like left (v320), named here as the next increment.
## v322 — the trail can name the COMMENT’S removal (M7/v322 — ADR 0016 amendment 325)

**The gap.** The service has recorded `uncomment` since v289 and the product has been able to do it since v321 (the un-comment’s door). The trail’s **filter** still could not ask for it: the verb was in the "All" bucket and in the summary’s own count, and there was no way to say "just the comments that were taken back". A filter that cannot name a recorded fact is a filter over a part of the trail — and the part it left out was the one the reader had just caused.
**The button.** A **Comment removed** button, asking in the trail’s own action (`uncomment`) — the same raw word every other button on that row has used since v281 — and the same rule: the label wears the display word, the filter asks in the action. This is the last of the two undoings the service records and the product can now do; the trail’s filter can name every verb the service writes for a publication.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§36).** The trail offers a filter for the comment removal → filtering by it shows ONLY the comment removals → and the SUMMARY still counts the whole trail. (The summary is the part that says what the filter is hiding — v281’s rule, and the one the desk keeps too.)
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the button), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+1 case), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §36).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1168/1168 (65 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — the service has recorded this verb since v289), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **227/227** with the new §36.
**Bounds (§60).** The trail’s filter row is now nine buttons wide and wraps onto two lines in a 400px panel: a filter row that grows without end stops being read, so the next verb to arrive should be folded into a group button (a "the social verbs" kind) rather than given a ninth and tenth line. And the filter is still a VIEW over the whole trail — the service’s `/audit` takes no action parameter, so the narrowing happens in the browser over a page the service already sent; moving it into the service is a door that does not exist yet, and it is named here as the next step (the same next step the desk’s reason filter waits for).
## v323 — the REPORTER sees the words THEY wrote (M7/v323 — ADR 0016 amendment 326)

**The gap.** The owner’s desk has shown the reporter’s words since v304 — a complaint without its words is a category, not a complaint — and the reporter’s OWN half did not. So a reporter opening the panel could see that their complaint existed, what reason it carried and where it stood, and not one word of what they had actually written. The wire has carried the words all along (`detail` ✓): the client’s row simply dropped them, and the panel had nothing to show.
**The row keeps the words.** `ReportRow` gains `detail` — the reporter’s own text, as written — and the reporter’s half shows it under the row, in the same muted style the owner’s desk uses for the same words. A report with no words has none, rather than a made-up one: the row’s value is an empty string, and the panel shows nothing where there is nothing.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§37).** The reporter’s own half shows the words they wrote → and they are the words the report was filed with (the same string the service holds — the one the earlier section already read back from the service).
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/reportBar.ts` (the row keeps `detail`), `apps/studio/src/lib/reportBar.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the reporter’s half shows them), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §37).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1170/1170 (65 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged — the wire carried the words already), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **229/229** with the new §37.
**Bounds (§60).** The words are shown to the reporter only — the artifact page’s report bar still shows the count and the reasons and not the words, because that page is public and a complaint’s wording is the reporter’s to say or not to say (the owner reads it on the desk, where it is the owner’s business). The row’s `detail` is now carried by every report row the client builds, including the bar’s rows — the bar’s renderer does not show it, and the words sit in the browser’s memory where the report already was: that is the same exposure the report itself always had, and not a new one. And the desk’s rows still do not LINK to the publication a complaint is about: an owner reading a complaint has to recognise the title, and a title is not the work — the next increment should make the title a link.
## v324 — the desk’s rows LINK to the work the complaint is about (M7/v324 — ADR 0016 amendment 327)

**The gap.** The owner’s desk showed the title of the complained-about publication and nothing else. A title is a NAME — a reader has to trust that it belongs to the work they are thinking of, and an owner with twenty works and a complaint in front of them has to recognise the name. The trail’s target has been a link since v272, for exactly this reason ("an audit row that named a project nobody could reach would be a row you could only take on trust"); the desk’s row was the one place that still asked the reader to take it on trust.
**The title is a link now.** The desk row’s title points at the artifact page of the publication the complaint is about (`/p/{targetProjectId}`, opened in a new tab, the same shape as the trail’s target). The row keeps everything it had — the title, the reason, the status, the reporter’s words, the Acknowledge button — so the only change is that the name is now something a reader can open.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§38).** The desk row carries a link → it points at the publication the complaint is about (the href IS `/p/{id}` of that publication) → and that page answers 200 with the Studio’s own document — read independently of the browser, because a link that 404s is worse than no link at all.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the title becomes a link, with a `data-desk-target` hook for the proof), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §38).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1170/1170 (65 files)** (unchanged — this increment is a component’s markup, and the component is graded by the real-browser proof), the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **232/232** with the new §38.
**Bounds (§60).** The link opens in a NEW TAB, so the desk panel is not thrown away while the owner looks at the work — the panel holds the state a reader came for (the tally, the filter, the reporter’s own half), and losing it to a click would be a small cruelty. The link is to the studio’s own artifact page (`/p/{id}`) and NOT to the service’s: the studio’s page is the one that carries the bars (the likes, the comments, the report link) and the one this product links to everywhere else. And the **reporter’s own half still names the publication as a bare id** — the same asymmetry this increment removed on the owner’s side — so the next increment should give the reporter the same link, or say why not.
## v325 — the REPORTER’S half links to the work as well (M7/v325 — ADR 0016 amendment 328)

**The gap.** v324 gave the owner’s desk a link to the work a complaint is about, and left the reporter’s own half naming the publication as a bare id. That asymmetry was not a principle: it was the order the two increments happened in. A reporter reading their own complaint has the same need as an owner — to see the thing the complaint is about — and the same reason not to be satisfied with a name: an id is a name the reader has to trust, and the reporter is the person who read that work.
**The same link, the same shape.** The reporter’s row names the publication as a link to `/p/{targetId}`, opened in a new tab — exactly what the owner’s row got one increment ago. Nothing else moved: the row still shows the reason, the status, the reporter’s own words and the Take-it-back button (v306).
**What the real-browser proof grades (§39).** The reporter’s own row carries a link → it points at the publication the complaint is about → and that page answers 200 with the Studio’s own document — read independently of the browser, for the same reason as the owner’s link.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the reporter’s row names the work as a link, with a `data-filed-target` hook for the proof), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §39).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1170/1170 (65 files)** (unchanged — this increment is a component’s markup, and the component is graded by the real-browser proof), the service’s social + moderation packages **136/136** (unchanged), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **235/235** with the new §39.
**Bounds (§60).** The reporter’s row still names the work by its **id** and not by its title, because the reporter’s wire (the filed list) carries the id and not the title — the owner’s desk gets the title because the owner’s wire carries it, and the difference is in the service’s two doors, not in the panel. Making them agree is a service change (a title on the filed report), and it is named here rather than done. The link opens in a new tab for the same reason as the owner’s (the panel holds the state the reader came for), and the reporter’s row keeps its own Take-it-back button, because a complaint the reporter can still withdraw is a complaint that is still theirs.
## v326 — the desk’s filter is the SERVICE’s (M7/v326 — ADR 0016 amendment 329)

**The gap.** v318 gave the desk a reason filter, and it was a VIEW: the whole desk came over the wire and the browser narrowed it. A creator with a thousand reports would download a thousand rows to see the spam ones — and the narrowing was in the wrong place, because the one that knows the reasons is the service. The v318 record named this as the next step ("moving the narrowing to the service is a door that does not exist yet"), and this increment is that door.
**The service narrows, in its QUERY.** `GET /moderation/reports?owner=&reason=` — and the narrowing happens in the finder (`findByTargetProjectIdInAndReasonOrderByCreatedAtDesc`), not after it. That distinction is the whole increment: the desk’s rows are a WINDOW (the most recent N), and a window taken BEFORE the narrowing would answer a different question than the one asked — "the most recent hundred reports, of which these are the spam ones" rather than "the most recent hundred spam reports". The cap has to reach the database either way, and the two questions must not be confused.
**A reason this build does not know is REFUSED BY NAME.** The reasons are a closed set of four (the social package’s own set — v295), and an unknown reason is a 400 that names them — not a reason that is quietly ignored. A caller that asked for a reason the service does not have and was answered with the whole desk would read the answer as "there was nothing of that reason", which is a different fact, and the one that matters.
**The TALLY is not narrowed.** It is a door of its own (v312) and it counts EVERY report against the work — which is exactly what lets a reader see what the filter is hiding. The panel’s filter buttons now come from the tally’s own keys, because the rows on screen are narrowed and reading the reasons off them would offer only the reason already chosen.
**The browser’s copy of the narrowing is GONE.** `filterDeskRows` and its four cases were removed: two mechanisms doing one job is how a filter starts disagreeing with itself, and the service’s answer is the truth. The panel asks again when a button is pressed, and renders what the service answered.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§40).** The desk offers the reason filter → the panel shows ONLY that reason → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: its own answer for that reason carries that reason and nothing else → and the TALLY still counts the whole.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportRepository.java` (the narrowed finder), `.../moderation/ModerationService.java` (`deskOf(owner, limit, reason)` and the reason rule), `.../moderation/ModerationController.java` (the `reason` parameter), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/moderation/ModerationServiceTest.java` (+3 cases, and the existing calls carry the new reason), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationServer.ts`, `apps/studio/src/app/api/moderation/route.ts`, `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationLane.ts` (the reason travels), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel asks again; the buttons come from the tally), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.ts` and `.../moderation.test.ts` (the browser’s narrowing removed), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §40).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **139/139** (the moderation package 19/19), `apps/studio` **1166/1166 (65 files)** (four cases fewer, because the browser’s copy of the narrowing is gone), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **239/239** with the new §40.
**Bounds (§60).** **The trail’s filter is still the browser’s**, and it has to be: the trail’s door is the service’s `/audit`, in the `ops` package, which is the parallel lane’s work — so the audit’s action parameter is named here as a door that exists in the plan and not in this build. The desk’s narrowing changed one thing a reader can see: the window is now the most recent N **of that reason** (v318’s browser-side filter narrowed the most recent N of all reasons) — which is the better answer to the question actually asked, and it is stated here because it is a change in what "the desk" means. And the desk’s rows are still a window over the reports, whatever the reason: a creator with ten thousand spam reports sees the most recent hundred of them, and the tally is the number that says ten thousand.
## v327 — the creator sees WHO backs their plan (M7/v327 — ADR 0016 amendment 330)

**The gap.** v310 gave the creator the other side of the relation — the subscriptions they RECEIVED — and the rows showed a plan, a price and a standing. **They did not show who.** A creator reading that list was reading a list of plans, and the question a creator actually has ("who is backing this, and who stopped?") had no answer on the page. The name was not missing from the data: the supporter’s CLAIM carried it, and the service threw it away on the way in.
**The row keeps the name, the way the follow row has since v288.** `SubscriptionEntry` gains `subscriber_handle` and `subscriber_assurance` — the same two columns `FollowEntry` has kept for the follower’s name since v288 — and the service writes the claim’s handle onto the row when the subscription is made. The wire’s `handle` and `assurance` slots have existed since v300 and were filled with **null** by the controller; they are filled with the row’s values now. Nothing about the wire’s SHAPE changed: a slot that was there and empty is now there and carries what it was always meant to carry.
**A claim with no handle leaves no name.** The columns are nullable, and the panel falls back to the account id — the same identity the trail names (v270’s finding: the service has no account table to resolve an id into a handle with). The panel never invents a name, and it never shows a blank: the service’s own case says a claim without a handle produces a row without one.
**The panel says it.** The creator’s received rows carry the subscriber’s name beside the plan and the price, and the studio’s economy wire type grew the two fields the service now fills. The client’s helper prefers the handle and falls back to the id — so a service that started filling the slot would make the panel say the supporter’s name without a single change here.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§41).** The creator’s received subscriptions are on screen → each row says WHO is behind it, by the NAME the supporter’s claim carried → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: it sends the supporter’s handle on the wire, and the panel shows that very name. (A follower’s name was already on the wire for the same reason — v288 — and this closes the same gap on the other side of the same relation.)
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionEntry.java` (the two columns, the constructor, the getters), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (the claim’s name goes on the row), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (the wire’s empty slots are filled), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the wire type), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the row names the subscriber), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+1 case), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the received row says who), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §41).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **141/141** (the subscription package 39/39), `apps/studio` **1167/1167 (65 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **242/242** with the new §41.
**Bounds (§60).** The name is a SNAPSHOT of what the supporter’s claim carried when they backed the plan, and it is a CLAIM — the service records it and verifies nothing (ADR 0018), exactly like the follower’s name in the follow row since v288. A supporter who changed their handle afterwards still appears under the name they backed with, and that is the honest reading of a fact about the past. The columns are **added** by `ddl-auto: update` (which adds columns but never drops constraints — the schema-bound work that needs a migration tool is the trail’s target kind and the subscription’s renewal, not this), and a subscription made BEFORE this increment has no name on its row: its creator sees the account id, which is the truth about that row. And the name is shown to the CREATOR only — the supporter’s own list still shows the plan and the price, because the supporter knows their own name.
## v328 — the creator sees WHEN a subscription was taken back (M7/v328 — ADR 0016 amendment 331)

**The gap.** v327 gave the creator the supporter’s NAME, and the standing said whether the subscription was still up. But a row said **that** it ended and never **when**: the `active` flag was a fact about the past, and an expired subscription knew its period’s end, while a CANCELLED one knew only that it was over. So a creator asking the question a creator actually asks — "who stopped backing me, and when" — had half an answer, and the missing half was the date.
**The row keeps the instant.** `SubscriptionEntry` gains `ended_at`, and the service writes the instant of the cancellation (`markCancelled(clock.instant())`) — the service’s own clock, because "when the supporter took it back" is a fact about a machine and not about a browser. The wire’s `Subscription` gains the matching `endedAt`.
**The ending is the CANCELLATION’s, and the column is nullable for that reason.** A standing subscription has no ending; one that merely ran out of its period ended on its `expiresAt`, which is a different date and a different fact. The service’s own cases pin both halves: a cancelled row carries an instant, and a row that was never taken back carries none. The panel shows the line only when there is one — a row that says nothing rather than guessing.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§42).** The creator’s received row says WHEN the subscription was taken back → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: it sends the same instant it wrote down, and the panel’s date is that very date.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionEntry.java` (the column, the constructor, `markCancelled`), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (the instant of the cancellation), `.../social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (the wire’s `endedAt`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (it is filled), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the wire type), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the row’s `endedText`), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the received row says when), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §42).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **143/143** (the subscription package 41/41), `apps/studio` **1169/1169 (65 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **244/244** with the new §42.
**Bounds (§60).** The column is **added** by `ddl-auto: update` (which adds columns but never drops constraints — the schema-bound work that genuinely needs a migration tool is the trail’s target kind and the subscription’s renewal, not this), and a subscription cancelled BEFORE this increment has no instant on its row: its creator sees that it ended and not when, which is the truth about that row. The instant is a **snapshot of the service’s clock**, so a creator and a supporter can disagree about it by the width of a network round trip — and the service’s own clock is the one that is written down, because it is the one that decided the standing. And nothing here renews anything: a subscription that was taken back still cannot be made again (the database’s uniqueness rule, v313’s bound), so this increment makes the ending VISIBLE without making it reversible — two different questions.
## v329 — the creator sees WHEN the support began (M7/v329 — ADR 0016 amendment 332)

**The gap.** v328 gave the creator the instant a subscription was **taken back** — and the panel had no way to say when the support **began**. So a relationship was one date out of two: a creator could see that a supporter stopped and not when they started, which is the same half-answer from the other side. The wire has carried `subscribedAt` since v300 — the panel simply never said it.
**The row says it, and says nothing when it cannot.** `SubscriptionRow` gains `startedText`, the instant the subscription was made, formatted in the same shape as the ending so a reader sees two dates in one style. A wire with no start says nothing: the row is null rather than a date the service never sent, because "when" that was never recorded is not the epoch.
**The panel shows both.** The creator’s received row reads "… @bob · since 2026-10-21T09:12:33Z · took it back on 2026-10-21T09:14:02Z" — the person, the beginning and the end, in one line.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§43).** The creator’s received row says WHEN the support began → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: it sends the same start it recorded, and the panel’s date is that very date.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the row’s `startedText` and the helper), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the received row says when it began), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §43).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1171/1171 (65 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **143/143** (unchanged — the wire carried the start since v300), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **246/246** with the new §43.
**Bounds (§60).** This increment is the CLIENT’s half of a fact the service has been sending since v300 — the start was never missing from the wire, only from the panel — and the record says so, because "the panel was not saying it" and "the data was not there" are different gaps and only one of them is a missing column. The start is the service’s own clock (the row’s `subscribedAt`), and so is the ending, so the two dates are measured by the same clock and can be compared with each other; a reader comparing them with a date in their own calendar is comparing two clocks, and the panel does not pretend otherwise. And the panel says the INSTANT, not "3 days ago": a relative date goes stale the moment it is written, and a subscription is a fact about the past that does not move.
## v330 — the supporter sees WHOSE plan they back (M7/v330 — ADR 0016 amendment 333)

**The gap.** v327 and v328 gave the CREATOR the supporter’s name and the two dates. The other side of the same relation was still addressed by its plan id alone: a supporter’s own list read "pro · ¥15.00 · Active", and a plan id is an ADDRESS — a supporter with two plans could not tell which address belonged to the person whose work they were backing. The name was not missing from the service: the PLAN row has carried the creator’s handle since v301 (the creator’s own claim), and the subscription simply did not copy it.
**The name is copied on, like the price.** `SubscriptionEntry` gains `creator_handle`, and the service copies the plan’s creator handle onto the subscription when it is made — the same snapshot rule the price follows, and for the same reason: what a supporter agreed to is a fact about the past. The wire’s `Subscription` gains the matching `creatorHandle`, and the service’s own case says the copy is the plan’s name and not something invented.
**A plan with no name shows the id, not a blank.** The creator’s claim might have carried no handle, and the client falls back to the creator’s account id — a creator is a PUBLIC identity (their name is in the byline of everything they publish), so an address is better than a hole, and the panel never invents a name.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§44).** The supporter’s own row says whose plan they back → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: it sends the creator’s name on the wire, and the panel shows that very name.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionEntry.java` (the column, the constructor, the getter), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (the plan’s name is copied on), `.../social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (the wire’s `creatorHandle`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (it is filled), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+1 case), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the wire type), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the row’s `creatorText`), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the supporter’s row says whose), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §44).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **144/144** (the subscription package 42/42), `apps/studio` **1173/1173 (65 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **248/248** with the new §44.
**Bounds (§60).** The name is a SNAPSHOT of the creator’s claim as it was when the subscription was made, and it is a CLAIM — the service records it and verifies nothing (ADR 0018), exactly like the supporter’s name on the other side (v327). A creator who changes their handle afterwards still appears under the name the supporter backed, which is the honest reading of a fact about the past. The column is **added** by `ddl-auto: update` (which adds columns but never drops constraints — the schema-bound work that genuinely needs a migration tool is the trail’s target kind and the subscription’s renewal, not this), and a subscription made BEFORE this increment has no name on its row: its supporter sees the creator’s account id, which is the truth about that row. And the supporter’s row still does not say what the plan BUYS — a plan is a promise of support, and this build has no billing to keep it (v313’s bound), so the row says what was agreed and not what was delivered.
## v331 — the recipient of a tip sees WHO sent it (M7/v331 — ADR 0016 amendment 334)

**The gap.** The tip’s service has kept the payer’s handle since v298 (the row’s `payer_handle`, the payer’s own claim) and the wire has carried it since then too. The **panel** was calling every payer "a supporter" and throwing the name away — a category where a person was available. So a creator reading the money that came to them could see the amount and the message and not who gave it.
**The lane stops dropping it.** The tips lane narrowed every tip to an amount, a message and a flag; it now keeps the payer’s name and the payer’s account id. The panel asks the same pure helper the economy module uses (`tipPayerText`), so the wording lives in one place: the name when the claim carried one, the account id when it did not, and "nobody" when neither is there — an address is better than a hole, and the panel never invents a name.
**An ANONYMOUS tip says "Anonymous" and shows no name at all.** That is the payer’s own choice about their own money, and the recipient is told the tip is anonymous rather than being shown a name the payer withheld. The unit cases pin all three branches, because the anonymous branch is the one that must never leak: a tip that is anonymous with a name on the wire still reads "Anonymous".
**What the real-browser proof grades (§45).** The recipient’s tip row names WHO sent it → and it is the tipper’s name and not a category → and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: it sends the payer’s name on the wire, and the panel shows that very name.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/tipLane.ts` (the tips keep the payer’s name and id), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the wire’s `handle`/`payerId`, the row’s `payerText`, and the exported `tipPayerText`), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel’s tip type and the row that names the payer), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §45).
