Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **157/157** (the subscription package 48/48), `apps/studio` **1225/1225 (67 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **285/285** with the new §65.
**Bounds (§60).** The totals are in CENTS and the panel reads them as CNY — the same as the tips’ total has always read them (v298) — and a creator whose plans carry two currencies would see them added; that gap is older than this increment and is named here rather than hidden. The totals count only the STANDING rows — an expired subscription is not a commitment (v308’s rule) and a cancelled one is not either — and nothing here renews anything (v313’s bound). And the panel still says the COUNT of the subscriptions (the whole table’s, v359) beside the per-period totals — the two are different facts and both are true — and the rows still say their own period (v367) and who is behind them (v327).
## v369 — the money says which CURRENCY it is in (M7/v369 — ADR 0016 amendment 357)

**The gap, named by v368.** v368 made the subscription totals per PERIOD and said out loud that the totals are in cents and the panel reads them as CNY — the same as the tips’ total has always read them (v298) — and that a creator whose plans carry two currencies would see them added. A plan may be offered in any currency the service accepts (the plan’s own `offer` takes one), so that gap was real: a creator with a CNY plan and a USD plan was told one number, and a number that adds two currencies is a number nobody can act on.
**The database sums BY PERIOD AND CURRENCY.** The two grouped sums now group by the period AND the currency (v368’s queries), and the wire’s `PeriodTotal` carries the currency the amount is in. A group without a currency is DROPPED rather than shown — a period with an amount and no currency would be a total in a currency nobody could name — and the client’s wording says each amount in its own currency: "¥15.00 per month + $30.00 per year".
**What the real-browser proof grades (§66).** The panel’s total is the service’s totals, and every total names the currency it is in. The check holds in the empty case as well: when nothing is standing the service has no groups and the panel says NO amount at all — not a zero, and not a separator with nothing after it (v363’s rule, v368’s).
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionRepository.java` (the sums group by period and currency), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (the groups carry their currency and the order is period, then currency), `.../social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (`PeriodTotal` gains the currency), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (the receipt names it), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (the cases follow the new shape and a new one says that money in two currencies never shares a group), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the reader keeps the currency and drops a group without one), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.ts` (the lane), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the wording: each amount in its own currency), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+1 case), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §66).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **158/158** (the subscription package 49/49), `apps/studio` **1226/1226 (67 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **287/287** with the new §66.
**Bounds (§60).** The TIPS’ total still reads every tip as CNY — the service writes CNY on every tip (the tip carries no currency argument), so there is nothing to mix there yet; if a tip ever grows a currency, that total has to be grouped the same way, and that is named here rather than left to be discovered. The totals count only the STANDING rows (v308’s rule) and nothing here renews anything (v313’s bound). And the panel’s per-period wording is the service’s own periods (once · month · year) — a plan with a period this build does not know is passed through as it arrived (v342’s rule) rather than labelled as one of the three.
## v370 — the economy panel says when its rows are a WINDOW (M7/v370 — ADR 0016 amendment 358)

**The gap.** v359 made the service count the subscriptions over the whole table and the panel say that count — and the panel’s ROWS are the service’s window (the two are different sizes, and the record said so). The panel never said so: a reader saw "3 subscription(s)" beside two rows and had no way to know the third was not on the screen. The like, tip and follow bars learned this in v350; the economy panel had not — and it is the panel where a creator’s own money is.
**The panel says it.** A pure helper — `subscriptionsWindowText(count, shown)` — says "showing the 2 newest of 3" when the list is shorter than the count, and nothing at all when the list is the whole thing (a note about a window that is not a window is noise) or when there is no list. Both the HELD side and the RECEIVED side say it — the two are the same kind of fact about the same kind of door.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§67).** The panel’s subscriptions are on screen, the count beside them is the SERVICE’s whole count — read independently of the browser — and a list shorter than that count SAYS SO. The check reads the rows and the count and refuses a panel that shows fewer rows than its own count without saying why.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the pure `subscriptionsWindowText` — the wording lives in one place), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (both sections say it), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §67).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1228/1228 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **158/158** (unchanged — the service already counted the whole table), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **289/289** with the new §67.
**Bounds (§60).** The note names the window’s SIZE ("the 2 newest of 3") — it never claims to know WHICH rows are missing, because the service’s window is the newest first and the rest are simply older. The panel still shows the service’s window and does not grow the read to match the count (a read of the whole table would be a table scan on every panel open). And the plan bar on the profile page carries no count beside its list — so it has nothing to contradict — which is why this increment is about the economy panel and not about that bar.
## v371 — a price says whether it is NOW or the price that was AGREED (M7/v371 — ADR 0016 amendment 359)

**The gap.** Two rows that look alike are facts about two different moments. A PLAN's row shows the terms the creator offers TODAY — and a creator may reprice a plan, period and all (v317). A SUBSCRIPTION's row shows the terms the supporter AGREED to — the subscription is a snapshot of the plan (v300). Nothing said which was which, so a supporter who saw the plan at ¥20.00 and their own subscription at ¥15.00 had no way to know that one was a price change and the other a promise. Worse, the same period was spelled TWO ways: the profile page's plan bar said "monthly" and the creator's own panel said "per month" for the same service's word — so a reader comparing the two could not tell they were the same thing.
**One wording, and every row says which kind it is.** The period now has ONE set of words everywhere (the panel's, which reads as money: "per month" · "per year" · "one time"; the bar's own spellings are gone — an unknown period is still passed through as it arrived — v342's rule). Every row then says which terms it is showing: a plan's row says "current terms", and a subscription's row says "as agreed". A RETIRED plan says no such thing — its terms are the last ones it offered, and calling them "current" would be wrong about it — and the totals, which sum the subscriptions' agreed prices, say it too.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§68).** The panel marks the SUBSCRIPTION rows as the terms that were agreed, the profile bar marks the plan as the terms on offer today, and the SERVICE agrees — read independently of the browser: the plan carries its own period and the subscription carries the agreed one, and the two are read apart.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the two notes · the rows carry them · the totals say it · the wording), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.ts` (ONE wording for a period — the bar now defers to the shared helper — and the bar's rows carry the note), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.test.ts` (the wording follows the one wording · +2 cases), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (both subscription rows and the plan rows say it), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §68 · the earlier check follows the one wording).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1233/1233 (67 files)**, the service's social + moderation packages **158/158** (unchanged — this increment is the client's honest reading of two facts the wire already carried: v367's snapshot period and the plan's own period), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **293/293** with the new §68.
**Bounds (§60).** The note says WHICH KIND of terms a row is showing, and nothing more: it does not say whether the plan's terms have MOVED since the subscription was made — the service does not compare the two, and a note that claimed a change it could not see would be inventing one. The note says nothing about money owed: nothing renews anything (v313's bound — one subscription per supporter per plan is the DATABASE's rule), so "as agreed" is a fact about a past agreement and not a bill. The totals count only the STANDING rows (v308's rule) and are in the service's own currency per group (v369), and the panel still shows the service's window beside the whole table's count (v370).
## v372 — the profile’s plan bar says how many plans there are, and that its list is a window (M7/v372 — ADR 0016 amendment 360)

**The gap.** The service’s plans door answered with a window (twenty at most) and a field called `count` — and the count was the WINDOW’s length, not the creator’s catalogue. So the number was a fact about the read, not about the creator, and the profile page’s plan bar never showed it at all. A creator who offers five plans and had three on the screen looked, to a reader, like a creator who offers three — and the bar had no way to say otherwise. The tip bar on the same page had learned this in v350; the plan bar had not.
**The count is the WHOLE table’s.** The repository gains `countByCreatorAccountId` (the whole table), the service gains `countPlans` (the creator’s account, checked the same way every other door checks one), and the door answers with that count — both when it lists plans and in the offer receipt. The client keeps the count the wire gave it: a wire with NO count says the count is UNKNOWN (null) rather than falling back to the window’s length — a count of zero would say the creator offers nothing, and the window’s length would be counting what happened to be on the screen.
**The bar says it.** The bar carries the count beside its list in the TIP BAR’s words (v350): "5 plans · showing the 3 newest of 5" — and nothing at all when the list is the whole thing, and no count line at all when the count could not be read. The count is the creator’s own catalogue — the number of plans they offer — and not what those plans have earned (the bar’s own rule, unchanged).
**What the real-browser proof grades (§69).** A creator offers TWENTY-ONE plans — more than the window of twenty — the SERVICE counts the whole table and the rows are the window’s, and the profile page says both: how many plans there are, and that its list is a window — read independently of the browser.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/PlanRepository.java` (the whole table’s count), `.../social/SubscriptionService.java` (`countPlans`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (both the door and the offer receipt), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/planServer.ts` (the count is `number | null` and a missing count is unknown), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.ts` (the facts carry the count and the bar says it), `apps/studio/src/app/u/[accountId]/route.ts` (the page hands the count to the bar), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.test.ts` (+3 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §69).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **160/160** (the subscription package 51/51), `apps/studio` **1236/1236 (67 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **296/296** with the new §69.
**Bounds (§60).** The count is the creator’s number of plans — retired ones included, because the bar’s rows show a retired plan too, and the two are the same set. The window is the service’s newest-twenty, and the note names its SIZE ("showing the 20 newest of 21") — it never claims to know which plans are missing, and it never grows the read to match the count (a catalogue of every plan on every profile visit is a table scan on every visit). And the count says nothing about what a plan has earned — that is the creator’s business and the bar’s own rule, unchanged.
## v373 — the economy panel’s plan list says how many plans there are, and that it is a window (M7/v373 — ADR 0016 amendment 361)

**The gap.** v372 taught the PROFILE page’s plan bar how many plans a creator offers and that its list is a window — and the creator’s OWN panel, one screen away, still showed the same plans with no count and no window note. So the creator who offered five plans saw three of them and had no way to know two more were there, while a reader on their profile page was told exactly that. Two screens, one service, two answers about the same list.
**One wording, both screens.** The wording moves into a shared helper (`planCountText` in `economy.ts` — "5 plans · showing the 3 newest of 5") and BOTH screens use it, so a reader who reads the profile bar and the creator’s panel reads one sentence → not two. The economy’s reader keeps the count the wire gave it: a wire with NO count says the count is UNKNOWN (null) rather than falling back to the window’s length — which would be counting what happened to be on the screen and calling it the creator’s whole catalogue — and a failed read says no count at all → not a count of zero → which would say the creator offers nothing → which is a different fact → ”the count could not be read” — v372’s rule, now on the creator’s side too.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§70).** The economy panel says how many plans the account offers, says its list is a WINDOW in the profile bar’s words, and the SERVICE agrees — read independently of the browser: the count is the whole table’s (the same creator who offered twenty-one plans in §69 — the page’s own list holds the window’s twenty — and the panel says both).
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the shared `planCountText` — the wording lives in one place), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.ts` (the bar uses the shared wording), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the plans’ count is `number | null` — a missing count is unknown), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.ts` (the lane carries the count), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel’s plan section says it), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyLane.test.ts` (+1 case), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §70).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1240/1240 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **160/160** (unchanged — the service counted the whole table in v372 — and this increment is the creator’s own panel reading that count), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **299/299** with the new §70.
**Bounds (§60).** The panel still shows the service’s window — twenty plans — and does not grow the read to match the count — and the note names the window’s SIZE, never which plans are missing — the service’s window is the newest first and the rest are simply older. The count says nothing about what a plan has earned — the creator’s own business — and the panel’s SUBSCRIPTIONS side still falls back to the window’s length when a wire carries no count — a gap older than this increment (v359’s wire always carries one, so it cannot bite today) and named here rather than left to be discovered. And the economy panel’s own plans are the account’s own — the same rows the profile page shows — so the two screens now say the same thing about the same list.
## v374 — the two sides use ONE pair of words: "as agreed" and "on offer now" (M7/v374 — ADR 0016 amendment 362)

**The gap.** v371 gave every row a note saying which kind of terms it was showing — but the two notes were not a PAIR: a plan’s row said "current terms" and a subscription’s row said "as agreed" →. "Current" says the wrong thing on both counts: a reader could read it as "this plan’s terms at this instant" → (when it is the CREATOR’s catalogue, and the plan is an offer that may be retired →) → and it does not pair with "as agreed" → (which is about a past promise →). A reader comparing the two rows was reading two unpaired sentences about two different moments.
**One pair of words.** The plan’s note is now "on offer now" — what is ON SALE — and the subscription’s stays "as agreed" — what was PROMISED →. One is about the present and one is about the past → and they are used together → wherever the rows are shown → (the profile page’s plan bar, the creator’s own panel, the supporter’s panel). A RETIRED plan still carries no note → its terms are the last ones it offered → and calling them "on offer" would be wrong about it →.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§71).** The creator’s own panel says the plan’s terms are ON OFFER now, the profile page says the same thing about the same plan, and the supporter’s panel says the subscription’s terms were AGREED — two different words for two different moments, read in a real browser.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the note’s words and the reason they changed), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.ts` (the bar’s own note of the same fact), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` and `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.test.ts` (the cases follow the new words), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §71 · the earlier check follows the new words).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1240/1240 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **160/160** (unchanged — this increment is a wording, and the wire is unchanged), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **302/302** with the new §71.
**Bounds (§60).** The words say WHICH TERMS a row is showing → and nothing more →: they do not say whether the plan’s terms have MOVED since the subscription was made → (the service does not carry the plan’s current price beside a subscription’s agreed one → and a note that claimed a change it could not see would be inventing one →), and nothing renews anything → (v313’s bound →). The panel still shows the service’s window beside the whole table’s count → (v370— v373’s windows) and the words sit beside the rows they describe →.
## v375 — the subscription’s row says the plan’s terms have MOVED since (M7/v375 — ADR 0016 amendment 363)

**The gap.** A subscription is a SNAPSHOT of a plan (v300): its price and its period are the ones the supporter agreed to. And a creator may reprice a plan, period and all (v317) — the subscriptions already made keep the terms they were made at, and the plan moves on. So a supporter could be holding a promise whose plan now costs more, and the row said nothing: the reader saw the OLD price and had no way to tell it was old. The pair of words (v374) said "as agreed" — which is true — but it never said that the OTHER half had moved.
**The wire carries both.** The subscription’s wire gains the plan’s CURRENT price and period (`currentPriceCents` · `currentPeriod`), filled from the plan rows themselves — read in ONE query for the whole window (the service’s `currentPlansOf`, not a query per row). A plan that is gone is simply ABSENT, and the wire then says nothing about the current terms rather than inventing them. The subscribe receipt carries them too — a receipt that said one thing and the list another would be two answers about the same promise.
**The row says it.** A pure helper (`planTermsMovedText`) says "the plan has changed: the price is now ¥25.00" — and the price and the period are read ONCE so the row and the note cannot disagree about the same facts. Terms that have NOT moved say NOTHING, and a plan that is gone says nothing (a note that guessed would be inventing a change nobody can see).
**What the real-browser proof grades (§72).** The panel says the plan has changed since the promise was made, and it names the price the plan costs NOW — and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: the wire carries the agreed price AND the plan’s current one.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionService.java` (`currentPlansOf` · `currentPlanOf`), `.../social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (the wire’s `currentPriceCents` · `currentPeriod`), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (both doors and the receipt), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the wire · the pure helper · the row’s `movedText` · the mapper), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the wire type), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (both rows say it), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+4 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §72).
Measured: the service’s social + moderation packages **162/162** (the subscription package 53/53), `apps/studio` **1244/1244 (67 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **305/305** with the new §72.
**Bounds (§60).** The note says the terms have MOVED — it does not say they moved in any particular way (a price may have gone UP or DOWN, and a period may have changed), and it does not say what the supporter owes: nothing renews anything (v313’s bound), so a moved price is a fact about the PLAN, not a bill. The plan’s currency never changes (the service’s reprice takes a price and a period), so the current price is written in the subscription’s own currency. And a plan that is gone says nothing — the reader is told the promise they hold and not a plan that is no longer there.
## v376 — the subscription’s row says when the plan is no longer ON OFFER (M7/v376 — ADR 0016 amendment 364)

**The gap.** A retired plan KEEPS its terms — the price and the period it was last offered at — and v375 put exactly those terms on the wire beside every subscription →. So a subscription’s row showed the plan’s CURRENT price and period → and the reader had no way to tell that the plan behind them was no longer for sale →: a retired plan’s terms look EXACTLY like an offer that is still running →. A supporter reading their own list could not tell that the plan they are backing is closed → and a creator could not tell it of the supporter whose promise they hold →.
**The wire says whether the plan is still offered.** The subscription’s wire gains `currentActive` — a BOOLEAN about the plan as it stands now — and the controller fills it from the plan row it already reads (the same one that carries the current price — v375’s). A plan that is GONE says nothing → that is a different fact → (the plan was never retired — it is not there →) — and the row already says what it can say about the promise →.
**The row says it.** A pure helper (`planOfferText` — "the plan is no longer on offer" —) is shown only when the plan is retired →, and both the HELD side’s rows and the RECEIVED side’s rows carry it → (the creator’s side matters as much: the promise they hold is against a plan that is closed →). A plan that is still offered says nothing → and a plan that is gone says nothing →.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§73).** The panel says the plan is no longer on offer, the note is on the ROW — not floating on the panel — and the SERVICE agrees, read independently of the browser: the wire says the plan is not on offer any more →.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/SubscriptionDtos.java` (the wire’s `currentActive` — a field, not a change), `.../social/SubscriptionController.java` (it is filled from the plan row already read), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the wire · the pure `planOfferText` · the row’s `offerText` · the mapper), `apps/studio/src/lib/economyServer.ts` (the wire type), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (both rows say it), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+4 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §73 · and the proof’s OWN budget: the Studio compiles its routes on demand, and a cold compile on a busy machine can outlast one wait — so the check reloads the page and probes again, and the check itself is unchanged: it still grades what the panel says — the machine this proof runs on is often busy with other projects →).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1248/1248 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **162/162** (unchanged — the controller reads the plan row it already had → and the wire’s new field is filled there →), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **308/308** with the new §73.
**Bounds (§60).** The note says the plan is no longer ON OFFER → and nothing about what that means for the promise →: a retired plan’s subscriptions keep standing → (v314’s rule — retiring the OFFER is not cancelling the PROMISES →) and nothing renews anything → (v313’s bound →), so the reader is told the offer is closed and not told that the promise died →. And a plan that is GONE says nothing → — the reader is told the promise they hold → and not a plan that is no longer there →.
## v377 — the creator’s own panel says the plan is RETIRED (M7/v377 — ADR 0016 amendment 366)

**The gap.** A retired plan KEEPS its terms →, so its row looks exactly like a live offer →, and the creator’s own panel said nothing about it →: the row carried the plan id, the price and the period → and NO button → — and a plan with a price and no button reads like an offer that is still running →. The profile page’s plan bar has said "retired" since v301 → — so the same plan was retired on one screen and alive on the other →.
**One word, two screens.** The wording moves into a shared helper (`planRetiredText` — "retired" —) and BOTH screens use it →: the profile page’s bar → and the creator’s own panel’s plan row →. A plan that is still offered says nothing → (an empty string → — and the row is not a hole →).
**What the real-browser proof grades (§74).** The creator’s own panel says the plan is retired — in the profile bar’s own word — and the profile page says the same thing about the same plan →.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.ts` (the shared `planRetiredText` — the wording lives in one place), `apps/studio/src/lib/planBar.ts` (the bar uses the shared wording), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel’s plan row says it), `apps/studio/src/lib/economy.test.ts` (+2 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §74), `docs/LONG_TERM_PLAN.md` (the landed row carries its own git version →).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1250/1250 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **162/162** (unchanged — the plan’s standing was already on the wire → and this increment is the two screens saying it the same way →), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **311/311** with the new §74.
**Bounds (§60).** The word says the plan is RETIRED → and nothing about what that means for the promises made against it →: a retired plan’s subscriptions keep standing → (v314’s rule — retiring the OFFER is not cancelling the PROMISES →) and nothing renews anything → (v313’s bound →). And the row still says the plan’s terms → the LAST ones it offered → (a retired plan keeps them →) — which is a different fact from the offer being closed → and is why v376’s note (on a SUBSCRIPTION’s row) and this one (on a PLAN’s row) say different things →.
## v378 — the desk’s line says its rows are a WINDOW (M7/v378 — ADR 0016 amendment 367)

**The gap.** The creator’s desk carried TWO counts of the same thing on one screen. The tally above counts EVERY report against the account’s work (v309 · v312 — the whole table’s), and the line under it said "{the window’s rows} report(s) · {the window’s open} still waiting" — the WINDOW’s counts — with nothing saying so. So a creator read "5 report(s)" in the tally and "3 report(s)" in the line and had no way to know which was the whole (and the "still waiting" number was the window’s too — the whole table’s was in the tally’s own text). The like, tip and follow bars learned this in v350; the desk had not.
**The line says the window.** A pure helper (`deskWindowText` — "showing the 3 newest of 5") says it, and the line carries the whole number’s owner: with no filter the whole is the tally’s total and with a reason filter it is that reason’s count in the tally’s `byReason` (so a filtered list is measured against the reports WITH that reason and not against the whole). A list that shows EVERYONE says nothing (the tally above already says how many there are) and an unknown total says nothing — a count of the window’s own length would be exactly the lie this increment removes.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§75).** The desk’s line says the WINDOW and never a count of its own, and the tally keeps the WHOLE number — both read in a real browser.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.ts` (the pure `deskWindowText` — the wording lives in one place), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the line says it and the whole it is measured against comes from the tally), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.test.ts` (+3 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §75).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1253/1253 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **162/162** (unchanged — the tally already counted the whole table and this increment is the panel saying which number is which), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **314/314** with the new §75.
**Bounds (§60).** The note names the window’s SIZE and never which reports are missing (the service’s window is the newest first and the rest are simply older). The desk still reads a WINDOW and does not grow the read to match the count (a desk of every complaint against every publication is a table scan on every panel open). And the tally keeps counting the WHOLE answer under a filter — that is v281’s rule for the trail and v318’s for the desk: a filter never hides anything without a number saying it is there.
## v379 — the trail’s tally says it counts the ROWS on the screen (M7/v379 — ADR 0016 amendment 368)

**The gap.** The trail panel’s tally said "4 published · 1 took down · …" and nothing about WHICH rows it had counted. The trail is a WINDOW — the service caps at a hundred and the panel asks for twenty — so the tally was a fact about twenty rows and it read like a fact about the whole trail. The panel’s footer did say "20 shown · 20 renderable · the service carried 20 · the service caps at 100" — but the tally sat beside it unqualified, and a reader who read the tally first had no way to know.
**The tally names its rows.** `trailSummaryText` takes the number of rows it is counting and says so — "of these 20 rows: 4 published · 1 took down" — and the panel hands it the rows it is showing. A caller that has no row count still gets the plain tally (the panel always has one) — so the wording is available without being forced.
**What the real-browser proof grades (§76).** The trail opens, its tally shows, and the tally says it counts the ROWS on the screen — "of these N rows" — read in a real browser. The earlier check’s NAME follows the truth too: it said "the tally of the whole answer" and it was never the whole answer; it is the tally of the rows it is showing.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.ts` (the tally names its rows — the wording lives in one place), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel hands the tally the rows it is showing), `apps/studio/src/lib/trail.test.ts` (+3 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §76 · the earlier check’s name follows the truth).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1256/1256 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **162/162** (unchanged — the service’s cap and count are already on the wire and this increment is the client saying which rows it counted), `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **316/316** with the new §76.
**Bounds (§60).** The tally still counts the ROWS IT WAS GIVEN — it is not the whole trail, and it does not claim to be — and the whole trail’s own count is the SERVICE’S to give, which is the ops package’s `/audit` door (the parallel lane’s work — named here rather than pretended). The panel still asks for twenty and does not grow the read to match the service’s cap (a hundred audit rows on every panel open is a table scan on every open). And the tally counts the window’s rows whether or not a filter is on — the filter narrows the LIST, not the tally — and the panel’s own footer says how many rows the filter admits.
## v380 — the reporter’s OWN list and the follower’s OWN list say how many, in all (M7/v380 — ADR 0016 amendment 371)

**The gap.** Two lists in the desk panel showed their rows and said nothing about how many there were: "YOU FILED" and "YOU FOLLOW". Worse, they were not equally silent — the reporter’s door answered with the WINDOW’s own length, which reads as a total and is not one (a reporter who filed thirty-seven complaints was told they had the window’s twenty), and the follower’s side had the opposite defect: the service has always counted the whole table there, and the browser lane dropped that number on the floor.

**The service counts the whole table.** `ReportRepository.countByReporterAccountId` + `ReportService.countFiledBy` answer "how many reports did this account file, in all", and the reporter’s door carries that number beside the cap it applied — so the count and the rows can be different sizes and both are true. The owner’s desk already had this shape; the reporter’s direction now has it too.

**The lanes stop dropping it, and the panel says it.** The count travels through `reportLane.ts` / `followLane.ts` to the panel, which states it beside the rows — "N report(s) filed · showing the N newest of M" and "N follow(s) · showing the N newest of M" — and says "you have filed no report" / "this device follows nobody" only when the WINDOW and the COUNT are both empty.

**What the real-browser proof grades (§77).** The reporter files a SECOND complaint (a different reason on the same publication, which the service allows); the service is then asked for ONE row and still answers with the count of every complaint the account filed — the difference between a window and a total is proved, not asserted — and the panel states that whole-table number. The follower’s side is graded the same way against the service’s own count.

**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportRepository.java` (the count), `.../social/ReportService.java` (`countFiledBy`), `.../social/ReportController.java` (the whole-table count on the wire), `apps/studio/src/lib/reportLane.ts` + `.../reportServer.ts` + `.../followLane.ts` (the count reaches the panel), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the panel states it), `apps/studio/src/lib/reportLane.test.ts` (+3 cases), `apps/studio/src/lib/followLane.test.ts` (+2 cases), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportServiceTest.java` (+2 cases), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (a new §77).

Measured: `apps/studio` **1261/1261 (67 files)**, the service’s social + moderation packages **164/164**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` **exit 0**, doc gate **exit 0**, the real-browser E2E **318/318** with the new §77.

**Bounds (§60).** The counts are the SERVICE’s counts — a `COUNT` over the whole table — and the panel still asks for a window beside them (it does not read the whole table into the browser). These two lists still have NO pager: a reporter with more complaints than the cap sees the newest ones and the number that says so, and the older ones are not reachable from this panel yet — named here rather than pretended. The withdrawal’s own status line still says exactly what the service said about it, and a refusal still names the rule that was broken. And the follower’s count is the service’s `followingCount`, which it has always sent — this increment only stops dropping it and says it out loud.

## v381+v382 — the reporter's own rows say WHEN they were filed, and the window can be paged past (M7/v381+v382 — ADR 0016 amendment 372)

**The gap.** The report row has carried its filed instant since v295; the reporter's own panel showed the reason and the status and dropped the date — the same "every date should be on the page" rule v341–v344 applied everywhere else, missed here. And "YOU FILED" is a window with no pager: the client lane began sending `offset`, but the service ignored it (the controller hardcoded `0`) and the lane's failure answers did not name the window they asked for.
**What was completed.** The controller reads and clamps the offset (a negative one is the first page, not a refusal — the window it names does not exist). The lane asks for the offset and echoes it in every answer, success and failure alike: a panel must know which window it is looking at even when the read failed.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/ReportController.java`, `apps/studio/src/lib/reportLane.ts` + `reportServer.ts` + `app/api/reports/route.ts` + `components/Toolbar.tsx`, `reportLane.test.ts` (+2).
Measured: studio affected files **90/90**, `tsc` exit 0, the service **240/240**.
**Bounds (§60).** The panel does not page yet — the lane and the door carry the offset, and a pager control is the next increment. A page past the end answers an empty window, which is the service's honest answer, not an error.

## v383 — the duplicate-report rule is TIME-AWARE: a report STANDS for a period, then lapses (M7/v383 — ADR 0016 amendment 373)

**The gap.** "The same account cannot report the same publication for the same reason twice" held forever: a publication that was since fixed stayed held down by a complaint about its old state.
**The rule.** A report stands for a month (as filed today) and expires at its own `createdAt + period`, read off the ROW — a row filed under one period expires by that period even after the policy moves. The addition is calendar arithmetic in UTC, not `Instant.plus` (an Instant has no months; a "month" of thirty days would expire a report filed on the 31st a day early, every time). An unknown period expires by the month rather than standing forever.
**The constraint is gone, and the cost is named.** "At most one STANDING report per (reporter, target, reason)" is a rule about a moving instant, and a UNIQUE index cannot express one — so the rule lives in the service. Two simultaneous filings can now both pass the check and both land; a database-level rule needs a trigger or a migration tool, and this build has neither. Named, not pretended.
**Files.** `ReportEntry.java` (the `period` column; the constraint's removal with its reasons), `ReportDtos.java` (the closed set of two), `ReportService.java` (`isStanding`), `ReportServiceTest.java` (+4: expired no longer constrains; one day short still STANDS; a year expires a year later; the row carries the period).
Measured: the service **240/240**, BUILD SUCCESS — and this increment also REPAIRED HEAD, which did not compile: the call sites had landed without their receiving halves.

## the SERVICE suite's verdict is a gated one (M2 — ADR 0016 amendment 376)

**The gap.** `mvn test` was a command a human ran; nothing asserted its totals. A suite that silently stopped collecting a class would read as a pass.
**The proof.** `docs/evidence/m7/service-suite-proof.mjs` digests the service sources, runs `mvn -o -B test`, asserts the digest did not move under the run, then grades the surefire totals — 0 failures / 0 errors / tests > 0 / exit 0 / every `*Test.java` on disk present.
Measured (2026-10-23, after amendments 372–374): **240 tests, 10 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors, BUILD SUCCESS**. The first run at the previous HEAD went RED and correctly so — the service did not compile.
**Bounds (§60).** The proof grades the totals, not the cases; the digest covers the service sources only.


## the filed list can be PAGED (M7 v386 — ADR 0016 amendment 379)

**The gap.** v382 put the offset on the wire — the service pages the filed read, the lane names the window in every answer, the route forwards it — and the panel still had no way to ASK for an older page. A reporter with thirty-seven complaints saw twenty and was told there were thirty-seven, with no way to reach the other seventeen. The pipe was built; the tap was missing.
**The page is part of the ask.** `filedOffset` state, a pager with "‹ Newer" and "Older ›", and `refreshFiled(offset)` re-reads with the page named — the same shape the desk's reason filter uses (v326). "Newer" is disabled on the first page (there is no page before the first), and "Older" is disabled exactly when the window already holds every report the service counted — a pager that offered a page with nothing in it would be asking a question whose honest answer is an empty window.
**And the window says WHICH rows it is.** v380's note said "showing the N newest of M", which was a fact about the FIRST page that read like a fact about the list. It now says "showing 21–40 of 37" — or, on a page past the end, "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window", which is the service's honest answer rather than an error.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the page state, the pager, the window-aware total), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (the filed section grades the pager's two invariants).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1282/1282 (67 files)**, `npx tsc -p apps/studio/tsconfig.json` exit 0. No service change: v382 built the door this increment knocks on.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); the new checks drive the same selectors the section already uses and are graded on the next machine that can. The page survives a withdrawal (the re-read keeps the reader where they were), but a withdrawal that empties the LAST page leaves the reader on an empty window with the honest note — rows are never deleted, so that state needs the table to shrink, which this build never does. And the pager pages the FILED list only; the desk's window (v326) and the trail's window (v385) are still unpaged.


## the filed read's offset means ROWS, and a test now pins the translation (M7 v387 — ADR 0016 amendment 380)

**The gap, found while building the pager.** v382's filed door takes an `offset` and the lane documents it as "the newest N of this account's reports, STARTING AT offset" — rows. The service handed that number straight to `PageRequest.of(from, asked)`, which reads its first argument as a PAGE INDEX: a caller asking for the rows from 40 on was answered with the rows from 800 on. With no test on the parameter and no caller that paged yet, the two meanings never met — and the first pager to meet them would have jumped twenty windows per click and called every page "past the end".
**The fix.** The offset stays ROWS on the wire (the name and the lane's words were the honest ones); the service translates rows into the pages Spring Data speaks: `PageRequest.of(from / asked, asked)`. An offset that is not a whole window lands on the page boundary at or before it — the only granularity a window has, and the window whose rows the caller asked about.
**Files.** `ReportService.java` (`reportsFiledBy`: the translation, with the reason in the comment), `ReportServiceTest.java` (+3: offset 40 with a window of 20 lands on page 2 — rows 40–59, not 800–819; offset 25 lands on the boundary at or before it; a negative offset is the first window).
Measured: the service **249/249**, BUILD SUCCESS; `apps/studio` **1282/1282 (67 files)**, `tsc` exit 0 — no client change was needed, because the pager (v386) had already been written in the semantics the wire promised.
**Bounds (§60).** The translation is page-granular: an offset of 25 answers rows 20–39, not 25–44. A pager that moves by whole windows never notices; a caller asking for an arbitrary row offset gets the window containing it, which is stated here rather than discovered later.


## the desk's window can be PAGED (M7 v389 — ADR 0016 amendment 382)

**The gap.** v326 narrowed the desk in the service and v378 made the window say it was a window, and the panel still asked for the newest N and nothing else. An owner with more complaints than the cap had no way to read the older ones; the filed list (v386) and the trail (v388) had already learned this, and the desk was the last window left.
**The page is part of the ask.** `GET /moderation/reports?offset=…` in ROWS — the same meaning the filed read's offset has, and the same rows-into-pages translation (v387's lesson, applied from the start rather than discovered by the first pager). The lane echoes the window it asked for, so two reads in flight cannot be mistaken for each other's answers; the Studio's own door forwards the offset with a negative clamped to the first window.
**Two honest endings, and one of them is a COUNT.** Unfiltered, the tally's whole total says exactly where the end is, so "Older" is disabled at `offset + carried >= total`. Filtered, the tally counts BY REASON over the whole table (v312) and is NOT narrowed — so the desk can say where a filtered window ends with a number somebody counted, which is the one thing the trail's filtered ending (v388) could not do. The fallback, when no tally could be read, is the trail's rule: a window that came back short of the cap IS the end.
**And the window line says which rows, past the first.** `deskWindowText(shown, total, offset)` keeps "the N newest of T" on the first window and gives the position past it: "showing 21–40 of 37" — v386's correction, applied to the desk. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end", not "no reports against your work" — a fact about one window must not be told as a fact about the whole desk.
**Files.** `ModerationService.java` (`deskOf(owner, limit, offset, reason)`: the rows-into-pages translation), `ModerationController.java` (the parameter), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderation.ts` (the page-aware window line), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationLane.ts` (the ask and the echo), `apps/studio/src/lib/moderationServer.ts` (forwarded), `app/api/moderation/route.ts` (forwarded, negative clamped), `components/Toolbar.tsx` (the page state, the pager, the filter reset, the empty-window note), `ModerationServiceTest.java` (+4: page 1 for offset 20; offset 40 is page 2, not page 40; a negative offset is the first window; the narrowed finder gets the same translation), `moderation.test.ts` (+4), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (the desk section grades the pager's two invariants and the door's empty answer past the end).
Measured: the service **255/255** (10 classes, +4), BUILD SUCCESS; `apps/studio` **1288/1288 (67 files)**, `tsc` exit 0; the trail, audit and social contract proofs all exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); the new checks drive the same selectors the section already uses and are graded on the next machine that can. The rows-into-pages translation is page-granular: an offset that is not a whole window lands on the boundary at or before it. And the filtered ending trusts the TALLY's per-reason count over the whole table, not the window — sound because the tally is a separate GROUPED read (v312), and named here rather than assumed.


## the following list gets its PAGER (M7 v390 — ADR 0016 amendment 383)

**The gap.** v380 gave the following list its whole-table count, and every other list had since learned to page — the filed list (v386), the trail (v388), the desk (v389) — and the YOU FOLLOW panel still asked for the newest N and nothing else. A reader who followed thirty-seven accounts was told there were thirty-seven, shown twenty, and had no way to reach the other seventeen.
**The page is part of the ask.** `GET /follows?follower=…&offset=…` in ROWS — the same meaning the filed read's offset has, and the same rows-into-pages translation (v387's lesson, applied from the start). The lane echoes the window it asked for, so a re-read after an un-follow lands on the page the reader is on; the Studio's own door forwards the offset with a negative clamped to the first window. The wire answer carries the cap that was applied — the field the filed read's answer already carried (v382).
**And the ending is a COUNT, not an inference.** The whole-table count rides on EVERY answer (v380), so "Older" is disabled exactly when the window already holds every follow the service counted — no "a short window is the end" reasoning needed, which is the difference between this list and the trail's filtered ending (v388).
**And the window line says which rows, past the first.** "the N newest of M" on the first window, "showing 21–40 of 37" past it — v386's correction, the third list to receive it. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end", not "this device follows nobody" — a fact about one window must not be told as a fact about the whole list.
**Files.** `FollowService.java` (`followingOf(follower, limit, offset)`: the rows-into-pages translation), `FollowController.java` (the parameter and the cap in the answer), `FollowDtos.java` (`FollowingAnswer` gains `limit`), `apps/studio/src/lib/followServer.ts` (forwarded), `apps/studio/src/lib/followLane.ts` (the ask and the echo), `app/api/follows/route.ts` (forwarded, negative clamped), `components/Toolbar.tsx` (the page state, the pager, the position-aware window line, the empty-window note), `FollowServiceTest.java` (+3: page 1 for offset 20; offset 40 is page 2, not page 40; a negative offset is the first window), `followLane.test.ts` (+2), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (§30 grades the pager's two invariants and the door's empty answer past the end).
Measured: the service **258/258** (10 classes, +3), BUILD SUCCESS; `apps/studio` **1290/1290 (67 files)**, `tsc` exit 0; `social-contract-proof.mjs` exit 0 — the follow's wire record gained a field and the proof still reads both sides as agreeing.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); the new checks drive the same selectors the section already uses and are graded on the next machine that can. The rows-into-pages translation is page-granular: an offset that is not a whole window lands on the boundary at or before it. And the following list is the follower's OWN side — the "who follows ME" direction (v292) is still unpaged, named here rather than quietly assumed.

**And the window line says which rows, past the first.** "the N newest of M" on the first window, "showing 21–40 of 37" past it — v386's correction, the fourth and fifth lists to receive it. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end", not "nobody has given this account anything" — a fact about one window must not be told as a fact about the whole list.

## the economy panel's three lists get their PAGERS (M7 v392 — ADR 0016 amendment 385)

**The gap.** v359 and v372 gave the economy panel's three lists their whole-table counts, and every other list had since learned to page — the filed list (v386), the trail (v388), the desk (v389), the following list (v390), the tips lists (v391) — and the subscriptions held, the subscriptions received and the plans offered still asked for the newest N and nothing else. A supporter holding thirty-seven subscriptions was told there were thirty-seven, shown twenty, and had no way to reach the other seventeen.
**The page is part of the ask.** `GET /subscriptions?…&offset=…` (both directions) and `GET /plans?creator=…&offset=…` in ROWS — the same meaning the filed read's offset has, and the same rows-into-pages translation (v387's lesson, applied from the start). The three lists page INDEPENDENTLY: the subscriptions held, the subscriptions received and the plans offered are three different questions about three different sets, and one page for all three would answer none of them. The three answer records carry the row each window starts at, so three reads in flight cannot be mistaken for each other's answers (v382's rule); the Studio's own door forwards the three offsets with negatives clamped to the first window.
**And the ending is a COUNT, not an inference.** The whole-table counts ride on EVERY answer (v359/v372), so "Older" is disabled exactly when the window already holds every row the service counted — the same honest ending every pager since v386 has had.
**And the window lines say which rows, past the first.** `subscriptionsWindowText` and `planCountText` both gained the offset: "the N newest of M" on the first window, "showing 21–40 of 37" past it — v386's correction, now on six lists. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end", not "this account backs no plan" — a fact about one window must not be told as a fact about the whole table.

**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); the new checks drive the same selectors the section already uses and are graded on the next machine that can. The rows-into-pages translation is page-granular: an offset that is not a whole window lands on the boundary at or before it. And these are the ECONOMY PANEL's lists — the profile page's plan bar (v292) still reads through its own lane (`planServer`) and is unpaged, named here rather than quietly assumed.


## the profile page's tip bar gets its PAGER (M7 v395 — ADR 0016 amendment 388)

**The gap.** v350 gave the profile page's tip bar its window line and v355 put the whole-table count on every answer, and every list since v386 has learned to page — the filed list, the trail, the desk, the following list, the tips lists (v391), the economy panel's three lists (v392). The profile page's tip bar still read the first TIP_LIMIT rows and nothing else: a creator given 150 tips had 100 of them on their page, was told there were 150, and could not reach the other 50. The service's /tips door has paged by rows since v391 and the lane has echoed the window it answered all along — the page itself was the only side that could not move.
**The pager is a LINK, because the page cannot act.** The profile page is server-rendered and holds no session (ADR 0018 is why), so its pager cannot be a button: the route — the layer that knows the URL — builds the other windows' URLs and the bar draws an <a>. At a boundary there is no page to offer and the control is a <span> with no href: a link that went nowhere would be a lie with an href. A bar given no URL builder draws no pager at all — a link it cannot build is a link it does not draw. The route reads tipOffset, separate from the gallery's offset, so the two windows page independently; the links it builds keep the reader's query and gallery page where they were.
**And the ending is a COUNT, not an inference.** "Older" is offered only when the NEXT window would hold something: the whole-table count rides on every answer, so a page the service has already counted as empty is a page the bar does not offer — the honest ending every pager since v386 has had, stated here as the next window rather than the current one.
**And the window line says which rows, past the first.** "the 100 newest of 101" on the first window, "showing 101–150 of 150" past it — v386's correction, the eighth list to receive it. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window", and the bar still states the whole count beside it: the empty window is the page's, not the account's.
**Files.** apps/studio/src/lib/tipBar.ts (TipBarFacts gains offset, limit and pageHref; the window line is position-aware; the pager and its styles), apps/studio/src/app/u/[accountId]/route.ts (the tipOffset parameter, the URL builder, the offset and the limit on the answer), apps/studio/src/lib/tipBar.test.ts (+7: the first window's line; a later window names its rows; an empty window past the end says so; the pager is links and the boundary is a span; Older is not offered when the next window would hold nothing; a bar with no builder draws no pager; a whole-table window gets no pager), tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs (§80 gives one account 101 tips — one more than the page's window — and grades the first window, the second window and a window past the end as HTML).
Measured: apps/studio **1304/1304 (67 files)** (+7), tsc exit 0; the audit, trail and social contract proofs all exit 0 — the tip answers gained fields on the client side only, and the proofs still read both sides as agreeing.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); §80 drives the same fetch-the-page shape the profile checks have used since v270 and is graded on the next machine that can. The pager is LINK-based by necessity, not by choice: a server-rendered page cannot flip a window without a round trip, and a button that did nothing would be worse than a link that goes somewhere. And this is the profile page's TIP bar — the plan bar and the follow bar on the same page still read through their own lanes and are unpaged, named here rather than quietly assumed.


## the profile page's plan bar gets its PAGER (M7 v396 — ADR 0016 amendment 389)

**The gap.** v372 gave the plan bar its whole-table count and its window line, and every list since v386 has learned to page — the filed list, the trail, the desk, the following list, the tips lists (v391), the economy panel's three lists (v392), and the tip bar on this very page (v395). The plan bar on the profile page still read the first window and nothing else: a creator offering 25 plans had 20 of them on their page, was told there were 25, and could not reach the other 5. The service's /plans door has paged by rows since v392 and echoes the window it answered — the page was again the only side that could not move.
**The pager is a LINK, for the same reason the tip bar's is.** The page is server-rendered and holds no session (ADR 0018), so the route builds the other windows' URLs and the bar draws an <a>; at a boundary the control is a <span> with no href, and a bar given no builder draws no pager at all. This is the page's THIRD window: the route reads planOffset beside the gallery's offset and the tips' tipOffset, and the links it builds keep the reader's query and their place in the other two windows where they were.
**And the answer names the window it is FOR.** PlansAnswer carries limit and offset: two reads in flight must not be mistakable for each other's answers (v382's rule), and a pager that cannot say which plans it is showing can only page from a guess. A negative offset is clamped to the first window — a question about a page before the first is a question about the newest plans, not a refusal.
**And the empty window is the page's, not the creator's.** A window past the end says "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window" and still carries the whole count beside it; only an empty FIRST window keeps the bar off the page, which is v301's rule and is still right about it. "Older" is offered only when the next window would hold something — the whole-table count rides on every answer, so a page the service has already counted as empty is never offered.

The page now carries THREE independent windows and the follow bar is the fourth list on it that still reads through its own lane and is unpaged — named here rather than quietly assumed. The "Older" rule is stricter than v392's was (a window the count has already emptied is never offered), and the economy panel's three pagers still use the older, looser rule — a difference this section records rather than hides.


## the profile page's follow bar gets its PAGERS (M7 v397 — ADR 0016 amendment 390)

**The gap.** v303 put both directions of the edge in one bar on the profile page, v390 taught the FOLLOWING direction to page, and the tip and plan bars on the same page got their pagers in v395 and v396. The FOLLOWERS direction never paged at all: a creator followed by 37 accounts had 20 of them on the page, was told there were 37, and could not reach the other 17. The service's followers door took a limit and always answered from the first row, so this was the one list on the page with no second page to offer — and the service had to learn it first.
**A read and a write are different questions.** The followers door's answer is its OWN record now, FollowersAnswer, carrying the limit the service applied and the row the window starts at. A WRITE's answer is not a window at all — the followers a follow carries are the edge it just made — and a window named on that would be a window of nothing. The service pages in ROWS, the same translation the following direction has used since v390 (v387's lesson: PageRequest speaks PAGES), and a negative offset is the first window rather than a refusal.
**One bar, two lists, two independent windows.** The bar pages BOTH of its lists, each on its own URL parameter (followersOffset, followingOffset), and each pager preserves the reader's place in the other four windows on the page. The window line is position-aware in the shared wording — "the 20 newest of 21" on the first window, "showing 21–21 of 21" past it — and an empty window past the end says "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window" while still carrying the whole count: the emptiness belongs to the PAGE, not to the account.
**And the pager is a LINK, for the reason it has been since v395.** The page is server-rendered and holds no session (ADR 0018), so the route builds the URLs and the bar draws an <a>; at a boundary the control is a <span> with no href, and a bar given no builder draws no pager at all. "Older" is offered only when the next window would hold something — the whole-table count rides on every answer, so a page the service has already counted as empty is never offered.

Measured: `apps/studio` **1335/1335 (68 files)** (+19), `tsc` exit 0; the audit, trail and social contract proofs all exit 0; FollowServiceTest 19/19 (+4) — the followers door gained a parameter on the service side and the proofs still read both sides as agreeing.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); §82 drives the same fetch-the-page shape the profile checks have used since v270 and is graded on the next machine that can. The page now carries FIVE independent windows and five URL builders that each preserve the other four — a URL that grows with every list that learns to page, recorded here rather than discovered later. And readFollowing still falls back to the window's length when the wire carries no count (v390's shape, unlike the plans answer's null, v372): the service has carried the count since v380, so the fallback is a path the real door does not take, and it is named rather than quietly assumed.


## every pager in the Studio ends on the NEXT window (M7 v398 — ADR 0016 amendment 391)

**The gap.** A pager has two controls and one honest question per control. "Newer" is disabled on the first window, because there is no page before it. "Older" is the interesting one, and the Studio had been answering it two different ways: v392 disabled it when the window ALREADY held every row the service counted, while v391 and every pager since (v395, v396, v397) offered it only when the NEXT window would hold something. The two rules agree on every full window. They disagree exactly when the service answered with fewer rows than the cap while the count says more rows sit past the next window's start — and there the older rule sends the reader to a page the service has already counted as empty. Nine pagers in the Toolbar were still on the older rule.
**One rule, in one place.** `olderWindowWouldHoldSomething(offset, windowSize, count)` in `apps/studio/src/lib/pager.ts`: rows exist at offsets 0..count-1, so "the next window would hold something" and "there is a row at offset + windowSize" are the same sentence — which is why the rule can never hide a reachable row, only stop offering a page that is known to be empty. A count that could not be read offers nothing rather than guessing (an unread count is not a count of nothing, v288), and a window with no size names no page.
**And nine pagers read it.** The trail (unfiltered), the tips received, the tips given, the subscriptions held, the subscriptions received, the plans, the desk, the filed reports and the following list — one sentence instead of nine, so the next pager that is added cannot quietly pick the weaker ending again.
**Files.** `apps/studio/src/lib/pager.ts` (new — the rule, with the case the two rules disagree on written out), `apps/studio/src/lib/pager.test.ts` (new, +7: the next window is offered when the count says rows sit past it; it is not offered when the count says it would be empty; a short window that is not the end still has a next window; a short window at the end offers nothing; an exact multiple ends at the boundary; an unread count offers nothing; a window with no size offers nothing), `apps/studio/src/components/Toolbar.tsx` (the nine `disabled` conditions and the six comments that described the old ending).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1342/1342 (69 files)** (+7), `tsc` exit 0; the audit, trail and social contract proofs all exit 0 — a change to when a button is disabled, graded by the suite that already reads every panel's window lines.
**Bounds (§60).** The trail keeps its heuristic on the FILTERED branch and the desk keeps its on the no-tally branch, because neither has a count of the set it is paging: the service's audit count is the WHOLE trail's, narrowed or not (v385), so a window that came back short of the cap remains the only honest ending available there. And the rule is about the COUNT's word, so it inherits whatever the count is worth: a count read a moment before the rows can disagree with them, and the pager then offers a page that has since emptied — the same race every windowed list here carries, and not one a pager can close.


## the artifact page’s comment thread gets its PAGER (M7 v399 — ADR 0016 amendment 392)

**The gap.** v286 made the artifact page’s comment thread a WINDOW (the service caps at a hundred comments) and v354 gave it a visible count, and every list since v386 has learned to page — the filed list, the trail, the desk, the following list, the tips lists (v391), the economy panel’s three lists (v392), the profile page’s tip, plan and follow bars (v395/v396/v397). The thread still read the first hundred rows and nothing else: a reader on a thread longer than the cap saw its head, was told how many comments the thread held, and could not reach the older ones. The service’s /comments door is the last big window on a public page that could not move.
**The row count is the end.** The window pages by ROWS, so the answer carries the thread’s ROW count, the removed ones included — not the visible count, which counts only the comments still standing. A thread of fifty comments of which thirty were taken back has thirty visible and fifty rows, and a pager that used the visible number as its end would stop a reader twenty rows short of it. The service takes an offset in rows and translates it the way every other windowed read here does: PageRequest speaks PAGES (v387’s lesson), so the rows from 40 on are page 2, and a negative offset is the first window rather than a refusal — the newest comments are the honest answer to a question about a page before the first. The answer is a SEPARATE record from the author direction’s, because that direction does not page and has no whole-table count: putting a window’s fields on its answer would name a window that was never asked for.
**And the window line says which rows.** "the 100 newest" on the first window, "showing rows 101–101 of 101" past it — v386’s correction, the wording the tip and plan bars received in v395 and v396. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window" and still states the whole count beside it: the empty window is the page’s, not the publication’s.
**And the pager is a LINK.** The artifact page is server-rendered and holds no session (ADR 0018), so the route — the layer that knows the URL — builds the other windows’ URLs and the section draws an <a>; at a boundary the control is a <span> with no href, because a link that went nowhere would be a lie with an href. "Older" is offered only when the NEXT window would hold something: v398’s one rule, shared with every other pager in the Studio, so the newest pager cannot quietly pick the weaker ending again.
**Files.** `services/nexus-api/src/main/java/dev/nexus/api/social/CommentService.java` (commentsOf takes an offset and pages by rows; CommentResult gains `total`), `CommentDtos.java` (the `ThreadAnswer` record), `CommentController.java` (the offset parameter, the window echoed on the answer), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentServer.ts` (readComments forwards and echoes offset/total), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentBar.ts` (the optional window argument — offset, total, pageHref — the position-aware window line, the pager, the styles), `apps/studio/src/app/p/[projectId]/route.ts` (the commentOffset parameter and the URL builder), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentBar.test.ts` (+10), `apps/studio/src/lib/commentServer.test.ts` (new, +6), `services/nexus-api/src/test/java/dev/nexus/api/social/CommentServiceTest.java` (+4), `tools/harness/studio-publish.mjs` (§83 gives one publication 101 comments — one more than the window the page reads — and grades the first window, the second window and a window past the end as HTML).
Measured: `apps/studio` **1358/1358 (70 files)** (+16), `tsc` exit 0; the audit, trail and social contract proofs all exit 0; CommentServiceTest 26/26 (+4) — the thread answers gained a field on the service side and the proofs still read both sides as agreeing.
**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); §83 drives the same fetch-the-page shape the artifact checks have used since v286 and is graded on the next machine that can. The page’s window is the SERVICE’s cap (a hundred), so a thread of a hundred and one is the smallest one that can show a second window at all — the harness gives exactly that, and the count and the window differ by one. The ROW count is the pager’s end and the visible count is not, which means a thread whose older comments were all taken back still pages to them: the rows exist, and the window line counts the removed ones among the rows it names. And the comments the AUTHOR wrote still do not page (v335’s direction), named here rather than quietly assumed.


## the artifact page’s like bar gets its PAGER (M7 v400 — ADR 0016 amendment 393)

**The gap.** v350 gave the like bar its window line and v336 named the likers, and the comment thread on the SAME page learned to page in v399. The bar still read only the first window the service’s default allowed: a publication liked by more accounts than the cap had its older likers on the page as a COUNT and nothing else — the reader was told there were more and given no way to see them. It was the last windowed list on a public page that could not move.
**The count IS the row count here.** Every other pager in the Studio pages by rows, and the like bar needed no second number to do it: a like is never soft-deleted — an un-like DELETES its row — so the whole count the bar has always carried is exactly the number a pager pages by. The comment thread’s answer (v399) had to carry a separate total because removed comments keep their rows; this one does not, and that difference is a fact about the verb rather than an inconsistency. The offset is ROWS past the newest likes, translated the way every other windowed read here does (PageRequest speaks PAGES, v387’s lesson), and a negative offset is the first window rather than a refusal.
**And the window line says which likers.** "the 20 newest of 21" on the first window, "showing rows 21–21 of 21" past it — v386’s correction, the wording the tip and plan bars received in v395 and v396. An empty window on a LATER page says "this page is past the end — the service answered an empty window" and still states the whole count beside it: the empty window is the page’s, not the publication’s — "nobody liked this" and "this page is past the likers" are different facts.
**And the pager is a LINK.** The artifact page is server-rendered and holds no session (ADR 0018), so the route — the layer that knows the URL — builds the other windows’ URLs and the bar draws an <a>; at a boundary the control is a <span> with no href, because a link that went nowhere would be a lie with an href. "Older" is offered only when the NEXT window would hold something: v398’s one rule, shared with every other pager in the Studio.

**Bounds (§60).** The real-browser E2E could not run here (no Docker, no Java service); §84 drives the same fetch-the-page shape the artifact checks have used since v286 and is graded on the next machine that can. The page’s window is the service’s DEFAULT (twenty), so a publication with twenty-one likes is the smallest one that can show a second window at all — the harness gives exactly that. The count is the pager’s end BECAUSE a like is deleted rather than marked: if a like ever learns a soft delete, the answer needs the comment thread’s second number, and this is where that would be noticed. And the VIEWER is still not sent from this page (the account lives in the browser’s storage, ADR 0018), so the page cannot say whether YOU liked it — v284’s bound, unchanged by the pager.

## the CHARACTER AGENT’s own rules, graded directly (M4/v401 — ADR 0016 amendment 394)

**The gap.** `packages/world-engine/src/characterAgent.ts` had no test file of its own. Everything it decides was reached only through `worldEngine.test.ts` — one whole-world suite that carried the agent along — so a rule could change and the suite would stay green because the world around it absorbed the difference. The crash v162 fixed (a rest action with no location) lived in exactly this blind spot.
**The proof.** `packages/world-engine/test/characterAgent.test.ts` (25 cases) asks the agent its rules directly: the action set (rest is unconditionally available — including for a locationless agent, v162’s crash point; move is priced by distance; co-located characters can talk; a DECLARED co-located NPC counts once; ground items offer take; the wounded offer heal; an active goal offers pursue), the decision (the chosen action is in the available set; alternatives ≤3 and exclude the choice; confidence in [0,1]; low HP or low energy picks rest; an agent with nothing still gets rest), the execution (rest heals to the cap; move changes location and writes the event; talking writes BOTH relationship sides, floor first time then +5; a declared bond keeps its name and length; take moves the item; an unavailable action is refused BY NAME), and the world informing the agent (knowledge can be added; goal progress clamps and closes the goal when complete; someone else’s goal does not move).
Measured: world-engine **75/75 (3 files)** (+25); `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json` exit 0. The recent-events cases assert by CONTAINMENT — the engine writes its own events too, so an exact array would grade the engine’s bookkeeping instead of the agent’s.
**Bounds (§60).** These are unit cases over the deterministic engine — no real model behind the persona (v144’s bound, unchanged). The agent’s rules are graded here; the world’s orchestration stays with worldEngine.test.ts, and the suite runs where world-engine’s suite runs.

## the RELATIONSHIP core’s own rules, graded directly (M4/v402 — ADR 0016 amendment 395)

**The gap.** v162 wrote the relationship arithmetic as pure functions and the engine’s tick has carried them ever since: a bond sat in a world, the world stepped, an assertion read the bond afterwards. That is coverage of the ENGINE and thin coverage of the RULES — the band edges were probed at six numbers, the custom `perTick`/`floor`/`gain`/`cap` parameters never, and the claims the module’s own doc-comment makes (“decay never manufactures hatred”, “a declared bond is never retyped”, “absence wears a bond back to met-once”) were prose rather than cases. The same shape v401 found in the character agent: a rule with no direct case is a rule the next refactor is free to break quietly.
**The proof.** `packages/world-engine/test/relationships.test.ts` (20 cases) asks the arithmetic directly. The constants are pinned — floor 10, cap 100, gain 5, decay 2 — and the roster is measured to be EXACTLY the four derivable types in band order, with the floor sitting ON the enemy/neutral edge and the cap inside the ally band. The bands are SWEPT, not spot-checked: every edge in its band, everything below the floor an enemy however far below, everything past the cap an ally, and a walk from −20 to 120 that never moves backward. Decay takes the step and the floor it is given, converges from the cap to the floor in exactly 45 steps and then stops moving, and no strength in [0, cap] decays into the enemy band — the doc-comment’s “never manufactures hatred”, as a loop rather than a sentence. Gain pays the declared step, caps at 100 from below and from above, and its one asymmetry is pinned honestly: a gain has NO floor, because the floor is written at first meeting by the agent, not enforced here. And the two halves compose: a bond tended every tick walks neutral → friend → ally on the arithmetic alone (friend at tick 10, ally at tick 15, capped at 18), and a bond neglected every tick walks ally → friend → neutral and rests at the floor.
Measured: world-engine **95/95 (4 files)** (+20); `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json` exit 0. The engine’s own suite (43) and the agent’s (25) are unchanged and still green — the new file adds a reader, not a rewrite.
**Bounds (§60).** These are the pure functions’ OWN rules — where the engine APPLIES them (the tick’s decay pass, a declared bond keeping its name) stays where it was graded (worldEngine.test.ts). The composed walks grade the arithmetic, not the engine’s policy of decaying every unmaintained bond every tick — that policy is the engine’s and is tested there. The constants are pinned because the rest of the engine QUOTES them (the agent seeds a first meeting at the floor); a future tuning that moves a number will redden this file on purpose, which is the point.

## the STUDIO STORE’s own authoring rules, graded directly (M1/v403 — ADR 0016 amendment 396)
