The OS pid is surfaced — the handle that makes killing a tree possible at all (amendment 55). A nonzero exit arrives as a CODE, not as an error. An argument carrying shell metacharacters (`;` `&` `|` `$` backtick quotes `<` `>`) arrives as ONE argv token — `shell:false`, nothing re-parsed, which is the property the whole argv-as-data design rests on. The env handed in crosses into the child verbatim. And `kill()` ends a never-ending child promptly — the handle the timeout budget relies on.
Measured: `npx vitest run test/nodeSpawn.test.ts` (agents/exec-host) → **6/6**.
**Bounds (§60).** What the OS does with a killed tree, and what a real subprocess does with a malformed argv, are the OS’s subjects; this file grades that the adapter asks for exactly the argv-as-data, `shell:false` spawn it claims to. The hermetic suite beside it keeps its fakes and its zero-real-processes property; this file is the ONE place real processes run, and it says so in its own header. And whether the adapter SHOULD carry `windowsHide` is a decision about console windows, not graded here — what is graded is that no shell ever sees the argv.

## the STAGED CHANGE ORDER, graded directly (M7/v469 — ADR 0016 amendment 462)

**The gap.** `STAGED_CHANGE_ORDER` is the order the staged tally is spoken in, and the tally case proves the SENTENCE comes out canonical — but the constant that decides it was never asked: its membership, that no kind is spoken twice, that the sentence’s order IS this list’s order whatever order the counts arrive in, and that every kind the parser can name is on the list at all. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, filtered into every sentence, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Four new cases in the same file (39 → 43) ask it directly. The order is the five git change kinds, in the canonical sentence order. No kind appears twice — a sentence that counted one kind twice would double-report it. The tally speaks in THIS order whatever order the counts arrive in: a reversed-insertion tally is pinned against an expectation COMPUTED FROM the constant, so the sentence and the list cannot drift apart. And every kind the parser can name is on the list — a five-kind diff is parsed and each kind it produces is asserted present, so the vocabulary and the sentence agree.
Measured: `npx vitest run test/gitStagedTool.test.ts` (agents/exec-host) → **43/43**.
**Bounds (§60).** Whether added-before-modified is the RIGHT order for a human is a decision about the sentence’s shape, pinned as measured, not argued. What git calls a change — and what a future git would add to its vocabulary — is git’s subject; this file grades that every kind THIS parser names is on THIS list. And the floor/AT LEAST wording around a trimmed read is v66’s subject; this file grades the order the parts are spoken in.

## the world-save probe’s VERSION STAMP, graded directly (M7/v470 — ADR 0016 amendment 463)

**The gap.** `STUDIO_WORLD_SAVE_SERVER_VERSION` is the name every probe answer carries so a client can tell which build answered. The probe cases read the state but never asked about the stamp: that it rides BOTH the available and the unavailable answer, that it names its own lane, and that the two disk lanes’ stamps are not the same word. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, stamped onto every answer, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Four new cases in the same file (12 → 16) ask it directly. The stamp rides BOTH answers — available or not — with the unavailable one forced by a root that walks out, so a client can tell which build spoke whichever way the disk went. The stamp names its own lane and its build: it carries `studio-world-save` and a trailing version. The two disk lanes’ stamps are DIFFERENT words — a client reading an answer can tell a world-save probe from a scene-save probe. And the stamp rides the PROBE, not the write/read answers — each answer keeps its own shape, and the stamp is not sprinkled where it does not belong.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/worldSaveServer.test.ts` → **16/16**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What a client DOES with the stamp — caching, compatibility checks — is the client’s subject; this file grades that the answer carries it on every branch. Whether the stamp’s wording is the right one is a naming decision, pinned as measured, not argued. And why the store was unavailable (the root that walks out) is the store’s own subject; the probe’s half is that it still answers, still stamped, when the store refuses.

The stamp names its own lane and its build: `studio-save-fs` plus a trailing version. The two disk lanes’ stamps are DIFFERENT words — the scene probe and the world probe cannot be mistaken for each other. Whether the stamp’s wording is the right one is a naming decision, pinned as measured, not argued. And why the store was unavailable is the store’s own subject; the probe’s half is that it still answers, still stamped, when the store refuses.

## the container lane’s WORKDIR ROOT, graded directly (M7/v472 — ADR 0016 amendment 465)

**The gap.** The container lane’s jail root rides as `STUDIO_CONTAINER_WORKDIR_ROOT` on the policy, the lane state, and every plan’s resolved workdir, and the probe cases read the state but never asked about the root itself — `/workspace` sat in the assertions as a literal while the constant went unnamed. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, carried by the policy, used as the `planExec` jail root, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Five new cases in the same file (8 → 13) ask it directly. The constant IS a posix container jail root — starts with `/`, carries no backslash, no `..` segment, no trailing slash — and the policy carries that very constant (one name, one value), with the policy still validating against it. A run with no cwd — or a cwd that is not a string — lands exactly at the root, and the docker argv is told so with `-w`. A relative cwd is jailed UNDER the root (`sub/dir` lands at `/workspace/sub/dir`, `.` at the root itself), never beside it. An escaping cwd is refused by name before any spawn — `../etc`, the backslash-folded two-dot escape, absolute `/etc` and the Windows-style `C:` path each answer `cwd refused`, plan nothing (`planned: false`, empty dockerArgs), and leave zero spawn calls behind. And the lane state states the pinned root on both branches, available or not.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/containerServer.test.ts` → **13/13**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** Whether `/workspace` is the right root INSIDE the image is a naming decision, pinned as measured rather than argued. What the container DOES under that root is the container’s own subject; this file grades that the request’s cwd can never leave it. And the docker client’s own cwd is a host-side concern the plan never sees; the jail is the container-side `-w`, which is what these cases read.

## the rotate grip’s HANDLE GAP, graded directly (M7/v473 — ADR 0016 amendment 466)

**The gap.** The rotate grip’s screen-space offset rides as `ROTATE_HANDLE_GAP` in both painters (`render.ts` and the canvas each divide it by zoom so the painted stem is constant on screen), and the rotate cases read the geometry but never asked about the constant — a literal `24` sat in the assertions while the exported number went unnamed. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, consumed by both painters, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Five new cases in the same file (18 → 23) ask it directly. The constant IS the pinned positive-integer screen gap, and the historical literal was this constant all along — both painted answers are now read through the name. At rest the grip sits exactly the constant above the top edge, on the centre line. The gap rides every rotation: a full 0–359° scan keeps the stem exactly the constant long. The constant is screen-space — divided by zoom, the painted stem is the constant at every zoom from 0.25× to 4×. And the gap does not scale with the node: every height from 4 to 1000 keeps the same constant stem.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/rotate.test.ts` → **23/23**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** Whether 24 px is the right affordance distance is a design decision, pinned as measured rather than argued. What the pointer DOES at the grip (drag thresholds, hit slop) is the interaction layer’s subject; this file grades the geometry the painters and the hit tests share. And zoom itself is the viewport’s subject; the gap’s half is that dividing by it keeps the SCREEN distance constant, which is what the scan proves.

## the publish lane’s DEFAULT LIST LIMIT, graded directly (M7/v474 — ADR 0016 amendment 467)

**The gap.** The publish lane’s default page size rides as `DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT` — the route falls back to it when a `list` action names no limit — and the listing cases asked with a literal `20` while the exported constant went unnamed. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, imported by the route as its fallback, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Five new cases in the same file (20 → 25) ask it directly. The constant IS the pinned positive-integer default. A listing asked with the constant reaches the listing door with exactly `?limit=20`. The constant is a fallback, NOT a cap — a caller’s own number rides through untouched whether it is 1 or fifty times the default. The answer’s limit is the service’s cap, never the constant — asked 10000, the service answers 100 and that is what the answer reports. And the constant composes with the owner window and the search door, riding the same query string on both doors.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/publishServer.test.ts` → **25/25**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** Whether 20 is the right page size is a design decision shared with the service, pinned as measured rather than argued. The route’s own fallback arithmetic (non-number, non-finite, fractional limits) is the route’s subject; this file grades that the constant it falls back to is a number the listing door accepts verbatim. And the service’s cap is the service’s subject; the Studio’s half is that it reports the cap the service named instead of trimming to its own default.

## the character agent’s DEFAULT CONFIG, graded directly (M7/v475 — ADR 0016 amendment 468)

**The gap.** The agent’s decision weights ride as `DEFAULT_AGENT_CONFIG` — the constructor merges every partial config over it — and the agent cases built agents with no config while the exported constant went unnamed. Measured on HEAD, the constant was exported, spread by the constructor, consumed by four of the six fields in the scorers, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Five new cases in the same file (25 → 30) ask it directly. The constant IS the full pinned shape: six named fields, each in its honest range. The three decision weights partition unity — a decision is a weighted vote, measured rather than argued. An agent built with no config runs exactly the default: its decision equals an explicit spread’s, field for field. The default survival weight is observable in the decision itself — a hurt character rests at exactly 5×survivalWeight/10 confidence. And one override changes one field — `goalWeight: 1` lifts heal past rest — while the shared default is never mutated by a construction and a later bare agent still decides by it.
Measured: `npx vitest run test/characterAgent.test.ts` (packages/world-engine) → **30/30**; `npx tsc -p tsconfig.test.json --noEmit` → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What the weights SHOULD be is a tuning decision, pinned as measured rather than argued. `decisionThreshold` and `planningHorizon` are pinned as declared shape; `decide()` does not read them yet, and that absence is the planner’s subject, not this file’s. And a config a caller PASSES is the caller’s subject; this file grades the default it falls back to and that a partial override moves exactly the field it names.

## the production WebGL backend, graded directly (M7/v476 — ADR 0016 amendment 469)

**The gap.** The seam (v108) split the renderer out so everything ABOVE it is testable without a GPU, and the headless half has had its own cases since v430 — but the WebGL half, the factory the runtime defaults to, had none: in Node a real `WebGLRenderer` cannot exist, so the factory’s own decisions were reachable only in a browser. Measured on HEAD, `createWebGLBackend` was exported, chosen as the runtime’s default factory, and named by no case.
**The proof.** Seven new cases across two files ask it directly. With the renderer faked ONE LEVEL BELOW the seam (the class the factory news up, five cases in a new file): the factory constructs the renderer with exactly the config’s renderer options — the canvas and the seven switches, nothing more; it applies the seven settings the runtime used to assign inline; it names itself `webgl`, hands the renderer out as `raw` (never undefined), and starts unsized with the config’s settings mirrored; `setSize`/`setPixelRatio` delegate to the renderer AND move the state they name; `render` and `dispose` delegate; and `stats` reads the driver’s own numbers verbatim rather than inventing them. Beside it, in the seam’s own file, the GL-less honesty (two cases): the production factory throws the real renderer’s error where no context exists — it never answers a headless backend in disguise — and the canvas it is handed is the canvas it uses, a probe error propagating from the real construction.
Measured: `npx vitest run test/renderBackend.webgl.test.ts test/renderBackend.test.ts` (packages/three-runtime) → **19/19**; `npx tsc -p tsconfig.test.json --noEmit` → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What a REAL GL driver does with the options is the driver’s subject; this file grades that the factory hands them over unchanged. The runtime’s choice of the WebGL factory as its default is the runtime’s subject; the factory’s half is what it builds and delegates when it is chosen. And a canvas that lies about its context is the canvas’s subject; the factory’s half is that it really constructs over the canvas it was handed, which the propagating probe error proves.

## the lane READS carry the budget the writes earned (M7/v477 — ADR 0016 amendment 470)

**The gap.** The comment and follow lanes gave every WRITE a 45-second signal (v294’s cold-route rationale) while both READS fetched with no signal at all: `readMyComments` and `readMyFollowing` could hang forever on a door that never answers, and the panel would sit there saying nothing — the exact failure the write budget exists to prevent. Measured on HEAD, `callSignal` existed in both files and was named by no case, and the read fetches carried no `signal`.
**The proof.** The constant is renamed `WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS` → `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (a budget that covers only the writes is a budget that believes only the writes can hang) and exported so a reader can see the one number the lane rides; both reads now pass `signal: callSignal()`. Eight new cases (16 → 20 and 17 → 21) grade it directly. Each read carries a live, un-aborted `AbortSignal`. The read’s budget is the pinned 45 seconds, read through the `AbortSignal.timeout` spy the v294 write case established. Each read gets its own fresh signal rather than a shared one. And one budget covers the whole lane — the exported constant, the read and the write all say 45 seconds.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/commentLane.test.ts src/lib/followLane.test.ts` → **41/41**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What the door DOES when the signal fires is the door’s subject; the lane’s half is that it asks with a deadline at all, and that the deadline is the one its writes already earned. The remaining signal-less reads (tipLane, likeLane, reportLane, moderationLane, auditLane and the service-side reads) are the same gap on other lanes and are named here rather than pretended away. And 45 seconds remains a measured decision, not an argued one.

**The gap.** The tip lane gave its write a 45-second signal (v294) while both reads — `readMyTips` and `readMyTipsGiven` — fetched with no signal at all: a door that never answered hung the tip history forever with no reason to show. Both reads carry a live, un-aborted `AbortSignal`. The reads’ budget is the pinned 45 seconds, read through the `AbortSignal.timeout` spy. Each read gets its own fresh signal. And one budget covers the whole lane — the exported constant, both reads and the write all say 45 seconds.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/tipLane.test.ts` → **20/20**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** Same shape as amendment 470: the door’s behaviour when the signal fires is the door’s subject; the lane’s half is that it asks with a deadline at all. The remaining signal-less reads (likeLane, reportLane, moderationLane, auditLane and the service-side reads) stay named rather than pretended away.

## the LIKE lane’s take-back and read carry the budget too (M7/v479 — ADR 0016 amendment 472)

**The gap.** The like lane’s LIKE rode the 45-second budget from the start, but the TAKE-BACK (a DELETE) and the READ fetched with no signal at all: a door that never answered hung the unlike forever and the likers list forever, with no reason to show. Measured on HEAD, `callSignal` existed, `likePublication` used it, and the other two calls named no budget.

Four new cases in the same file (9 → 13) grade it directly. The take-back and the read both carry a live, un-aborted signal. And the exported constant names the one number the whole lane rides.

## the report, moderation and audit reads carry the budget (M7/v480 — ADR 0016 amendment 473)

**The gap.** The read-side budget series reaches the last three lanes. `reportLane`’s filed-reports READ and its take-back DELETE both fetched with no signal — and the lane’s own file had pinned that absence as measured (“the next cycle can close it or defend it”); `moderationLane`’s `readDesk` fetched with no signal; and `auditLane` was the only lane that read with no deadline AT ALL, no constant and no helper beside it. The pinning case that recorded the take-back’s missing budget now asserts the signal instead of its absence — the gap it named is closed, not defended. Twelve new cases (20 → 24, 16 → 20, 13 → 17) grade it directly. Each read and the take-back carry a live, un-aborted signal. The budget is the pinned 45 seconds on every call of each lane, spy-witnessed. Each call gets its own fresh signal. And the exported constant names the one number.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/reportLane.test.ts src/lib/moderationLane.test.ts src/lib/auditLane.test.ts` → **61/61**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The service-side reads (`planServer`, `reportServer`, `followServer`, `auditServer`) carry their own `serviceCallSignal` discipline and are the next named surface, not this file’s. What a door does when the signal fires remains the door’s subject; the lane’s half is that it asks with a deadline at all.


## the service-side reads carry the budget (M7/v481 — ADR 0016 amendment 474)

**The gap.** v337 gave the Studio's WRITES the service's 15-second budget; the READS beside them — readPlans (v372), readSubscriptions (v449), readReports (v405), readFiledReports (v382), readFollowing (v390), readAuditTrail (v280) — fetched with no signal at all, so a service that never answered left the server-rendered plans, subscriptions, reports, filed-reports, following and audit panels waiting forever, with no sentence and no reason. Measured on HEAD, `serviceCallSignal` existed and the writes used it; six read calls carried no `signal`, and planServer's own file had pinned the subscriptions gap a fourth time (v337/v441/v442/v445).
**The proof.** The six reads now pass `signal: serviceCallSignal()`. The pin that recorded the subscriptions read's missing budget now asserts the signal instead of its absence — the gap it named four times is closed, not defended. Twelve new cases (53 → 57, 21 → 24, 20 → 23, 6 → 8) grade it directly. Each read carries a live, un-aborted signal. The budget is the pinned 15 seconds on every call, spy-witnessed through `AbortSignal.timeout` — twice in planServer, four times in reportServer, once each in followServer and auditServer. Each call gets its own fresh signal. And the refreshed sweep (window widened to 20 lines) reads 62 fetch call sites in `apps/studio/src/lib`, zero without a budget.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/planServer.test.ts src/lib/reportServer.test.ts src/lib/followServer.test.ts src/lib/auditServer.test.ts` → **112/112**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The read-side budget series is complete on every surface the sweep enumerates — every lane and every service-side door in `apps/studio/src/lib` now asks with a deadline. What a door DOES when the signal fires remains the door's subject; this series' half is that it asks with a deadline at all. A door the sweep does not enumerate (a new lane, a new fetch outside this folder) stays named rather than pretended away.

## when the budget FIRES, every lane says the door was LATE (M7/v482 — ADR 0016 amendment 475)

**The gap.** v477–v481 gave every lane and every service-side door a deadline, but nobody had pinned what the reader sees when the deadline FIRES. Measured on HEAD, all ten budgeted lanes caught the thrown error and answered with its RAW message: a fired `AbortSignal.timeout` surfaces the PLATFORM's string — "The operation was aborted due to timeout" — which names neither the door nor the budget, and cannot tell a reader whether the door is gone or merely late. The publish lane had named this distinction in v294 and the service door in v337, but the publish lane's TIMEOUT branch itself had no witness, and the other nine lanes had no distinction at all.
**The proof.** Each lane gains an `unreachableReason(error, door)` helper beside its `callSignal`: a `TimeoutError` answers "the <door> did not answer within 45 seconds" — the lane's own door name, the pinned budget — and every other error keeps its own message exactly as before. All 24 catch sites across the nine lanes ride it; `planLane` names its own two doors. Twenty-eight new cases grade it directly. Each lane's fired budget names its own door and the 45 seconds, never the platform's string. A door that is GONE still answers with its own message, never the lateness sentence. Every call of every lane rides the same distinction. A real `DOMException` from an `AbortSignal` is read the same way — the branch keys on the name, not the class. The publish lane's v294 timeout branch is pinned at last. And the fired budget keeps the answer's shape: a refusal that still names the window it was asked for, never an invented empty list.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/{auditLane, commentLane, economyLane, followLane, likeLane, moderationLane, planLane, reportLane, tipLane, publishLane}.test.ts` → **220/220**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What the UI does with the lateness sentence — a retry affordance, a "still compiling" hint — is the panel's subject; the lane's half is that it SAYS the door was late rather than leaking a platform string. The 45 seconds remains a measured decision (v294), not an argued one. A door outside these ten lanes that catches its own errors keeps whatever honesty it already has and is named by the sweep, not by this file.

## the last hand-rolled unreachable sentences name the lateness (M7/v483 — ADR 0016 amendment 476)

**The gap.** v337 gave the service-side doors a shared `serviceUnreachableReason` that tells a fired budget ("did not answer within 15 seconds") from a gone service ("unreachable at …"), v481 routed the six reads through the budget, and v482 gave every browser lane the same distinction. Measured on HEAD, THREE service-side doors still hand-rolled the pre-v337 sentence in their catches: `auditServer` (1 site), `reportServer` (4 sites) and `publishServer` (5 sites, at its own 45 seconds). A fired budget on any of them was answered as a service that was GONE — "nexus-api unreachable at …: The operation was aborted due to timeout" — the exact lie the v337 helper was written to end, and publishServer's own comment had been saying so since v294.
**The proof.** `auditServer` and `reportServer` import and ride `serviceUnreachableReason`; `publishServer` gains a local `publishUnreachableReason(apiBase, error)` at its own `PUBLISH_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` — the publishLane precedent of a door with its own budget keeping its own sentence. Seven new cases grade it directly. Each door's fired budget names the service's address and the budget — 15 seconds for the two 15-second doors, 45 for the publish door — and says neither "unreachable" nor the platform's string. A service that is GONE keeps the unreachable sentence with its own message, byte for byte. And every door of each file rides the same distinction: four in reportServer, one in auditServer, five in publishServer.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/reportServer.test.ts src/lib/auditServer.test.ts src/lib/publishServer.test.ts` → **65/65**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The budget series is now whole on every enumerated surface: every door asks with a deadline, and every door names the deadline's firing. What the UI does with the lateness sentence is the panel's subject. A door the sweep does not enumerate keeps whatever honesty it has and is named by the sweep, not by this file.

## the five Studio-route lanes the sweep never saw carry a budget (M7/v484 — ADR 0016 amendment 477)

**The gap.** The read-side budget series (v477–v481) and the firing-naming series (v482–v483) both worked from a sweep that counts `fetchImpl(` call sites — and five lanes call the global `fetch` DIRECTLY, because they predate the injected-fetch lanes: `buildLane`, `containerLane`, `deployLane`, `saveFsLane` and `worldSaveLane`. Measured on HEAD, all fourteen of their call sites carried no signal at all: a route that never answered hung the panel forever with no sentence and no reason, and their catches answered "studio server unreachable: …" for a door that had merely never been asked with a deadline. The sweep's own Bounds had named this shape ("a door the sweep does not enumerate stays named rather than pretended away"); this amendment closes it.
**The proof.** Each lane gains the house's budget block — exported `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 45_000`, `callSignal()`, and an `unreachableReason(error)` that keeps publishLane's v294 sentence: a `TimeoutError` answers "the Studio did not answer within 45 seconds", any other error keeps "studio server unreachable: <message>" byte for byte. All fourteen call sites now pass `signal: callSignal()`. Twenty new cases grade it directly. Every call of every lane carries a live, un-aborted signal, a fresh one per call. The budget is the pinned 45 seconds, spy-witnessed on `AbortSignal.timeout`. A fired budget names the Studio's lateness on every call, never the platform's string. And a Studio that is GONE keeps the unreachable sentence with its own message, as the pre-existing pins demanded.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/{buildLane, containerLane, deployLane, saveFsLane, worldSaveLane}.test.ts` → **68/68**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The budget is now whole across every enumerated door AND the five the enumeration missed — the honest statement is that a sweep is only as wide as its pattern, and the pattern now covers both `fetchImpl(` and the global `fetch`. What the panel does with the lateness sentence is the panel's subject. 45 seconds remains a measured decision (v294).

## the probe doors answer a 200 they cannot read honestly (M7/v485 — ADR 0016 amendment 478)

**The gap.** A JSON scan over `apps/studio/src/lib` (the sweep's sibling: `.json()` sites without an inline catch) found eighteen sites, seventeen of them already guarded — and six that were not: the PROBE doors of `buildLane`, `containerLane`, `deployLane`, `saveFsLane`, `worldSaveLane` and `publishLane` parsed their 200 body with a bare `(await res.json()) as XProbe`. A body that could not be read threw into the catch and was answered "studio server unreachable: …" — a false sentence about a server that had just answered, and the exact shape the house's rule about failed reads refuses. Measured on HEAD, two of the six files pinned that falsehood as measured.
**The proof.** Each probe now parses in its own try and answers "<door> route answered 200 with a non-JSON body" — the sentence every other door of the same file already uses — with every field at its empty default. The two pins that recorded the lie are rewritten to assert the honest sentence, each naming the increment that closed it. Four new cases grade the previously unpinned probes directly. A 200 with an unreadable body names the route and the status and never says "unreachable". And the answer keeps its shape: availability false, every list empty, every count zero.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/{buildLane, containerLane, deployLane, saveFsLane, worldSaveLane, publishLane}.test.ts` → **94/94**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** What the panel SHOWS for an unreadable body is the panel's subject; the lane's half is that it says the route answered badly rather than that the route is gone. The seventeen already-guarded `.json()` sites keep their guards and are named by the scan, not re-graded here. A route that answers 200 with JSON of the WRONG SHAPE is a different question — a cast, not a crash — and stays named rather than pretended away.

## the probe doors refuse a 200 that is JSON but not their shape (M7/v486 — ADR 0016 amendment 479)

**The gap.** v485 closed the unreadable body; the READABLE-BUT-WRONG one was still a bare cast. Measured on HEAD, the six probes (`buildLane`, `containerLane`, `deployLane`, `saveFsLane`, `worldSaveLane`, `publishLane`) passed their parsed 200 body straight through as the promised type. The dock reads `build.steps.length` and `build.steps.map` without a guard, so a 200 whose body was JSON of the wrong shape — `steps` missing or not an array — would crash the panel; a cast is not a contract, and a crash is the one answer this house refuses.
**The proof.** Each probe gains a shape validator beside it and refuses a wrong-shaped body with "<door> route answered 200 with a body that is not the probe's shape", every field at its empty default. The validators check the full declared shape — booleans are booleans, strings are strings, `recordCount` is a finite number, and `steps`/`rules` are arrays of arrays of strings. Six new cases grade it directly. A wrong-shaped 200 names the route and the status and never says "unreachable". The answer keeps its shape — availability false, every list empty, every count zero. And a right-shaped 200 still passes through untouched, as the pre-existing pins demand.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/{buildLane, containerLane, deployLane, saveFsLane, worldSaveLane, publishLane}.test.ts` → **100/100**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** The validators check the probe's OWN declared shape, not the service's full contract — what the service means to send is the service's subject, and the route tests own that. A panel that renders a partial probe is still the panel's subject; the lane's half is that it never hands the panel a thing its type says it is not. The POST doors of these files parse into their own answers and keep their existing shape discipline.

## the six intent readers are graded directly (M7/v487 — ADR 0016 amendment 480)

**The gap.** A class census of `apps/studio/src/lib` (every non-test module against its test file) named six modules with no test of their own: the intent readers — `commentIntent` (v286/v321), `followIntent` (v288), `likeIntent` (v284), `planIntent` (v301), `reportIntent` (v296), `tipIntent` (v298). They are pure and tiny, and that is exactly why they were never graded: every rule they keep is a rule one malformed link can violate, and the rule that matters most — a URL that names an EMPTY intent names NO intent — protects a composer from opening over nothing and a follow from being asked of the service in this device's name.
**The proof.** Six new test files grade the class directly, five cases each. Each reader reads its own key out of a `?`-prefixed search. Each accepts a bare query with no leading `?`, the shape a hand-built link may carry. A blank or whitespace value is NO intent — `null`, never an empty string. An absent key is null, and the other verbs' keys are not this verb's intent — `?unlike=x` is not a like. And the value arrives URL-decoded, because a link is allowed to encode its id.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/lib/{commentIntent, followIntent, likeIntent, planIntent, reportIntent, tipIntent}.test.ts` → **30/30**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** No source changed: this amendment grades what the readers already do, and every case passed on the first run — the record is that the class was UNTESTED, not that it was wrong. What the Studio does with an intent (which composer opens, which verb fires) is the component's subject; the reader's half is that it answers the URL honestly. The claim re-export (`likeClaimOf`) is `socialClaim`'s subject and is graded there.

## the comments ROUTE, graded directly (M7/v488 — ADR 0016 amendment 481)

**The gap.** A census of the Studio's own doors found eighteen of nineteen `app/api/**` routes with no test of their own — every lane's route layer was deferred to "the route suites' subject" by one Bounds after another, and that subject was never written. The comments route is the richest of them: a GET with three ask-refusals and a POST with six, each in the house's own vocabulary, and none of them graded anywhere.
**The proof.** A new route test grades the hop the lib tests never saw, with the service faked at the `fetch` boundary (the route calls the lib's defaults, so a stub grades it without any source seam). Fifteen cases. A missing or whitespace author is refused before the service is asked, with the sentence "the question names whose comments they are". The author is trimmed before the ask, and the padding never reaches the service. A limit that is not a number falls back to 20 — NaN is never forwarded. The lib's refusal becomes a 502 carrying the lib's own reason, never a 200 with an empty list. The six POST refusals run in order and each names its own rule — not JSON, not an object, no projectId, "a blank body is not a comment", and twice the author rule. The claim travels exactly as the browser built it, absent fields null rather than invented. And the service's own answer passes through: 201 with the comment, 503 with the reason.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/app/api/comments/route.test.ts` → **15/15**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** No source changed: the route was UNTESTED, not wrong, and every case passed on the first run. The other seventeen routes are the same class on other doors and are named here rather than pretended away. What the service does with a forwarded comment is the nexus-api suite's subject; this file grades what the Studio asks, refuses and forwards.

## the reports ROUTE, graded directly (M7/v489 — ADR 0016 amendment 482)

**The gap.** v488 graded the comments route, the richest of the eighteen untested doors the census named. The reports route is the next-richest and the same class: a GET with two ask-refusals, a POST with six, a DELETE with two more — eleven refusals in the route's own vocabulary, and not one of them graded anywhere. Every lane's route layer had been deferred to "the route suites' subject" by one Bounds after another, and that subject was never written.
**The proof.** A new route test grades all three verbs directly, the service faked at the `fetch` boundary (the route calls the lib's defaults, so a stub grades it without any source seam). Twenty-seven cases. The GET refuses a missing or blank reporter BEFORE the service is asked — "the reports an account filed need that account to be named". The reporter is trimmed before the ask, and the padding never reaches the service. A limit that is not a number falls back to 20, a limit of ZERO is the default window rather than a window of nothing, and a fractional limit is truncated rather than rounded. A NEGATIVE offset is clamped to the first page — a window before the first row is the newest rows — and a real one is forwarded as asked. The lib's refusal becomes a 502 carrying the lib's OWN reason, never a 200 with an empty list. The POST refusals run in order and each names its own rule — not JSON, not an object, no projectId, "a report needs a reason: one of spam, harassment, illegal, other", and twice the reporter rule. The claim travels EXACTLY as the browser built it, absent fields null rather than invented, and a detail that is not a string is forwarded as empty rather than invented. The SUBJECT is not trimmed: the route trims the account it is asked about, not the thing reported. And the service's own answer passes through: 201 with the report, 503 with the reason. The DELETE refuses a missing or blank reportId and a missing claimer, trims both ids before the ask, answers 200 when the report MOVED and also 200 when it was already withdrawn — not an error, and the route does not make it one — and passes the service's own 403 words through as a 502.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/app/api/reports/route.test.ts` → **27/27**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** No source changed: the route was UNTESTED, not wrong, and every case passed on the first run. The other sixteen routes are the same class on other doors and are named here rather than pretended away. What the SERVICE does with a filed report is the nexus-api suite's subject; this file grades what the Studio asks, refuses and forwards.

## the tips ROUTE, graded directly (M7/v490 — ADR 0016 amendment 483)

**The gap.** v489 graded the reports route. The tips route is the next door in the same class and carries a rule the others do not: ONE door serves TWO directions — the recipient's view of what they were given and the payer's receipt of what they gave (v332) — and nothing graded which side a question names, or what happens when it names both.
**The proof.** A new route test grades both directions directly, the service faked at the `fetch` boundary (the route calls the lib's defaults, so a stub grades it without any source seam). Twenty-three cases. A question naming NEITHER side is refused BEFORE the service is asked — "the question names who received the tips or who gave them", and two blanks are the same refusal. A question naming the recipient asks the recipient side, and one naming the payer asks the payer side — the receipt finally has a place to be read. A question naming BOTH is answered from the RECIPIENT side: the door serves the recipient first. A limit that is not a number falls back to 20, a NEGATIVE offset is clamped to the first window, and a real offset is forwarded as asked. The door trims NOTHING — a whitespace recipient is forwarded exactly as asked, and the service's empty window is the honest answer. The lib's refusal on the recipient side is a 503 that names the HTTP NUMBER, while the payer side carries the service's OWN words — an asymmetry the test pins rather than smooths over. An ok answer is a 200 carrying the tips, the total the DATABASE summed and the cap, and NOT the page. The POST refusals run in order and each names its own rule — not JSON, not an object, no recipientAccountId, no amountCents, and twice "a tip needs an account to be from". The claim travels EXACTLY as the browser built it, absent fields null rather than invented. A fractional amount is FLOORED — cents are whole. A message that is not a string is forwarded as empty rather than invented. Only a LITERAL true is anonymous: a truthy string is a named tip. And the service's own answer passes through: 201 with the tip, 503 with the reason.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/app/api/tips/route.test.ts` → **23/23**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** No source changed: the route was UNTESTED, not wrong, and every case passed on the first run. The other fifteen routes are the same class on other doors and are named here rather than pretended away. What the SERVICE does with a forwarded tip is the nexus-api suite's subject; this file grades what the Studio asks, refuses and forwards.

## the likes DOORS, graded directly (M7/v491 — ADR 0016 amendment 484)

**The gap.** The likes relation is TWO doors, not one: GET and POST at `/api/likes`, and the take-back at `/api/likes/[projectId]` — a dynamic segment, because a route that reads a path parameter has to BE one (v294). Neither door had a test of its own, and between them they keep rules no other route keeps: the `liked` flag is computed by the ROUTE from the rows the service answered, a like answers 200 rather than 201 because it is not a new row every time, and the path segment is DECODED before the ask.
**The proof.** Two new route tests grade both doors directly, the service faked at the `fetch` boundary (the routes call the lib's defaults, so a stub grades them without any source seam). Twenty-five cases. The GET refuses a missing or empty projectId BEFORE the service is asked, trims nothing (a whitespace projectId travels exactly as the browser asked it), asks the service with no viewer when none is named, and answers `liked: false` rather than guessing. With a viewer the route answers `liked` from the rows it was handed — true when the viewer is among the likes, false when not — and the service is never asked a second question. A limit that is not a number falls back to 20. The lib's refusal is a 503 carrying the lib's OWN reason, and a 200 that is not the likes shape is a refusal rather than a count of zero. An ok answer is a 200 carrying the count and the likes, and NOT the window the lib answered with. The POST refusals run in order and each names its own rule — not JSON, not an object, no projectId, and twice "a like needs an account: the service cannot tell one anonymous like from the same one twice". The claim travels EXACTLY as the browser built it, absent fields null rather than invented. And a like answers 200, not 201: liking twice is not two rows. The DELETE refuses a missing or blank claimer before the service is asked, forwards the claimer UNTRIMMED, decodes the path segment before the ask (a lowercase `%2f` arrives at the service as the canonical `%2F`), and passes the service's own answer and refusal through. A path segment that is not a valid escape THROWS — the door guards the claimer and nothing else, and the test says so rather than pretending a guard exists.
Measured: `npx vitest run --root apps/studio --no-file-parallelism src/app/api/likes/route.test.ts "src/app/api/likes/[projectId]/route.test.ts"` → **25/25**; `npx tsc --noEmit` (apps/studio) → exit 0.
**Bounds (§60).** No source changed: both doors were UNTESTED, not wrong, and every case passed on the first run. The other thirteen routes are the same class on other doors and are named here rather than pretended away. What the SERVICE does with a like is the nexus-api suite's subject; these files grade what the Studio asks, refuses and forwards.
